We saw this claim made by Liz Greene, and you will see similar statements made by Stephen Arroyo and other modern humanistic astrologers. We have already established that this position is a caricature of traditional astrology, and we have repeatedly seen that the meanings of Saturn in traditional astrology are very complex and mixed.
术语malefic 必须译“凶星”
malefic 判断的是一颗行星通常更倾向造成损害、分离、衰退或痛苦，并不等于 evil「道德之恶」。因此全书锁定「凶星」，不用「恶星」。本章正是这一译名的论证现场：若译成「恶星」，作者随后反驳「凶＝恶」时，中文会先替对方承认错误前提。
现代占星学很大一部分很难真正承认，占星学或整个人生中有任何事物可以被标为有害。人们有一股近乎迷恋的驱力，要为星盘中的一切找到正面解释。这种动机扎根于相信思想具有创造现实的一般力量。把事件标为负面或有害，危险似乎在于自我实现预言：人们害怕，只要指出星盘中的任何负面内容，当事人就会形成负面心态、对它产生负面思想，最终使它显现。
Much of modern astrology has a hard time really owning that anything in astrology, or in human life in general, can be labeled as harmful. There is a drive amounting to an obsession to find a positive interpretation for everything in the chart. The motive for this is rooted in the belief in the general power of thoughts to create reality. The danger of labeling an event negative or harmful is that of self-fulfilling prophecy, the fear that if you point to anything negative in their chart then a person will develop a negative mindset, think negative thoughts about it, and thus cause it to manifest.
我在正向思考的世界里长大，后来把这种现象称为「负面思考的压倒性力量」；它是任何正向思考哲学黑暗丑陋的阴影。人会对一切与人生有关的负面念头感到某种恐惧，害怕仅仅容许自己想到它们，就会帮助保证这些坏事成真。（我记得有本自助书名叫《你负担不起一个负面念头的奢侈》。）这会造成一种局面：你开始害怕自己自然出现的思想与感受，在内心打出各种死结，产生种种问题。
In the world of positive thinking, which I was raised in, I came to label this The Overwhelming Power of Negative Thinking, and it is part of the dark and ugly shadow of any positive thinking philosophy. There is a sort of terror of having negative thoughts about anything to do with our lives, in the fear that the very act of entertaining them will help to guarantee that these negative things come to pass. (I can recall a self-help book with the title, You Can't Afford the Luxury of a Negative Thought.) This can create a situation where you become afraid of your own natural thoughts and feelings, creating all sorts of internal knots and problems.
一个全然正面、没有负面的世界不可能存在。就是如此。相信人的思想如此万能强大，实在是一种压倒性的狂妄，是人类的骄傲。事实是，人类思想根本不是全能的。原书 p.180
It is impossible to have a world that is completely positive without negative. Period. The belief that human thought is this almighty and powerful is really a kind of overwhelming hubris, human pride. The truth is that human thought is just not omnipotent.
这种正向思考心态反映了我们文化对保持正面态度的狂热，也与西方文化过去几个世纪经历的一段惊人成长繁荣期相连。正向思考哲学的根源可追溯到十九世纪后期，那正是一个科学与技术前所未有地成长扩张之时代的中心。
This positive thinking mindset reflects our cultural mania about keeping a positive attitude. This is tied in with our Western culture experiencing an amazing streak of growth and prosperity over the past few centuries. The roots of positive thinking philosophy go back to the late 1800's, the center of an era of unprecedented scientific and technical growth and expansion.
过去半个世纪里，这种持续成长经验逐渐转向它的反面，进入文化衰退期；但实际条件的转换，尚未伴随相应的态度转换。我们依然像一个年轻成长的文化那样思考，即便这早已不再符合周围现实，而且已经不符合很久。条件变化与态度变化之间存在滞后；但我认为，我们正在抵达迫使态度转变的意识危机点。
This experience of continued growth has been gradually shifting in the past half century to its opposite, a period of cultural decline, but the shift in actual conditions has not yet been matched by a shift in attitude. We are still thinking like a young growing culture even though that no longer matches the reality around us, and it hasn't matched it for quite awhile now. There has been a lag between the change of conditions and a change of attitude, but I think we are reaching a crisis point in awareness that forces a shift in attitude.
我们需要找回平衡。
We need to get our balance back.
我们已经失去平衡与对立面的完整观念；若要清醒地驾驭今天周围的现实，就必须找回它。吉星与凶星是占星学赖以建立的核心对立组之一；此外还有昼与夜、阳性与阴性、主动与被动，以及许多其他配对。
We've lost the whole notion of balance, of opposites, and we need to recover that to sanely negotiate the reality around us today. The concepts of benefic and malefic are among the core sets of opposites that astrology is built upon, along with day and night, masculine and feminine, active and passive, and many other pairs.
吉星benefic建立、成长，令人愉快，会增建、扩张，把事物拉到一起。凶星malefic拆毁、枯萎、缩小或腐朽，常令人不快，会破坏、收缩，把事物拉开。这样把两者放在一起考察，就会看出它们显然是相互平衡的对立面，也是完整周期的不同阶段。
A benefic builds up, grows, is pleasant, builds, expands, pulls things together. A malefic tears down, withers, shrinks or decays, is often unpleasant, tears down, contracts, pulls things apart. Consider them together in this way and it is obvious that they are balancing opposites, and they are also different phases of a complete cycle.
同时容纳吉星与凶星，会把平衡带回世界。我们会经历两者，两者也都必要。只有其中一方，就像永远吸气而从不呼气，只有白昼没有黑夜，只有夏天没有冬天。原书 p.181
Allowing for both benefic and malefic brings balance back into the world. We experience both, and both are necessary. Having one without the other would be like perpetually inhaling without ever exhaling, having day without night, having summer without winter.
可以看到，吉星与凶星同已经讨论过的平衡概念相关。我们的文化把完美成功、正面、富有、强大、永葆青春等等当成理想。更成熟的世界观则以平衡为理想，强调培养复原力，也就是应对命运任何摆动的能力。
We can see that benefic and malefic are related to the concept of balance we have already talked about. In our culture it is an ideal to be perfectly successful positive, rich, strong, ever young, and so on. In a more mature world view, an ideal of balance emphasizes developing resilience, the ability to deal with any kind of swing of fortune.
我们的世界会发生好事，也会发生坏事。有时健康，有时患病；有些人似乎蒙受健康之福，另一些人却接连遭遇健康问题与受苦。个人生命的不同时期也会变化。有好运期与坏运期，有健康期与疾病期，有喜乐与悲伤期，也有快乐与受苦期。凡事都有定时。
In our world good things happen, and bad things happen. Sometimes you're healthy, sometimes you're sick. Some people seem to be blessed with good health, others have a string of health problems and suffering. This also varies by period in our individual lives. There are good streaks and bad streaks, streaks of health and illness, streaks of joy and of sorrow, streaks of pleasure and suffering. To everything there is a season.
我们的文化大体失去了这种感觉：起伏都是人生自然、必要而可欲的部分。我们忘记，往往正是在低谷与困难时期，收获某些最重要的长期回报。这属于我如今所谓的好运与坏运悖论：若考虑两者对品格的长期作用，坏运常能帮助成长，过多好运反而会在身体与道德上使人软弱腐败。
Our culture has largely lost the sense that ups and downs are all a natural, necessary and desirable part of life. We forget that it is often the down and difficult periods where we reap some of the most important long-term rewards. This is part of what I now think of as the paradox of good and bad luck; when we consider their long term effect on our character, often bad luck can help us grow, while too much good luck can weaken and corrupt us both physically and morally.
可以看出，吉星与凶星概念还与其他一些需要考察的重要概念相关，其中包括命运，也包括我们如何面对恶。
You can see that the concepts benefic and malefic are related to some other important concepts that we need to consider. These include the concept of fortune, and also how we deal with evil.
所有这些概念彼此交织。由于我们的心智如此运作，我们需要分别详细考察各个概念，同时记住它们属于一张相互关联的观念网络。
All of these concepts intertwine. Given how our minds work we need to examine the concepts in detail separately while remembering that they are part of an interrelated net of ideas.

恶Evil

没有人愿意碰这个主题，我也一样。历史上最伟大的心灵与人物已经为恶的问题挣扎数千年，我不敢自称握有答案；只能从自己的心与头脑出发来说。原书 p.182
No-one wants to touch this topic, including me. I don't claim to have the answers to the problem of Evil when the greatest minds and hearts of history have wrestled with this question for thousands of years. I can only speak from my own mind, and my own heart.
我想先把这个词的不同含义理清。
I want to start by sorting out the different meanings of the term.
一种恶是「不幸、有害」意义上的恶，也就是造成伤害的事件。按此义，癌症是恶，龙卷风摧毁房屋也是恶。还有道德之恶moral evil：人以自由意志蓄意选择伤害，意在造成伤害。那是远为黑暗复杂的问题。人有自由意志，能够选择；这就意味着，选择蓄意伤害的可能性永远存在。
There is evil in the sense of unfortunate, a happening that causes harm. In this sense, cancer is evil, and a tornado that destroys a house is evil. There is also moral evil, deliberate harm chosen by free will, with intent to cause harm. That is a far darker and more complex problem. As humans we have free will. We can choose, and that means there is always the possibility of choosing to cause deliberate harm.
术语harmful evil 与 moral evil
前者说一件事客观造成伤害，不必包含意图；后者才指行动者明知而选择伤害。中文分别用「有害意义上的恶」与「道德之恶」。这一区分也解释了为何凶星可以表示疾病灾害，却不能直接被称作道德邪恶。
可以用许多方式解释为什么恶事会发生在一个人身上。这些解释也能用于仅仅表示坏运的恶；但这里我要采用更极端的道德之恶。
There are many ways to try and explain why something evil happens to a person. These explanations can also be applied to evil just in the sense of bad fortune, but I want to use the more extreme case of moral evil here.
解释恶的一种方式，是诉诸业力或报应的道德律：若坏事发生在你身上，那就是你今生较早时候或前世所作之事的反作用与偿还。这是相当流行的现代解释，至少乍看似乎提供了合理答案。然而，我越思考这种选择，就越觉得不满意。它看似解释，其实什么也没有解释；只是把问题往后推，并从当事人意识经验之外的领域追究其责任——一个他没有记忆、也无法控制的领域。这等于直截了当地说：无论何时有坏事发生在你身上，都是你的错，是你应得的。
One way to explain evil is to appeal to a moral law of karma or retribution, that if something bad happens to you it is a reaction or payback for something you did earlier, either in this life or in a previous life. This is a fairly popular modern explanation, and at least at first glance it does seem to offer a reasonable answer. However, the more I think about this option, the less satisfactory I find it. It is an explanation that doesn't really explain anything; it just kicks the can down the road, and holds the person experiencing evil responsible for it from a realm of experience outside of their conscious experience, a realm where they have no memory and no control. It is the equivalent of bluntly saying that whenever anything bad happens to you it is your own fault, you deserved it.
很容易看出这种说法会怎样被滥用。一名女性遭到暴力强奸？那一定是她自己的错——责怪受害者。
You can see that can be easily abused. A woman was violently raped? It must have been her own fault - blame the victim.
流行业力观确有一部分真理：我们的生活方式会在进一步经验中产生后果，无论是今生、死后还是其他世。它固然有用；但过于轻易地用业力解释凶性事件，有严重局限。原书 p.183
There is some truth to the popular notion of karma, that how we live our lives does have consequences in further experiences we have, either in this life or after death or in other lives. Useful as that can be, there are serious limitations to using karma to explain malefic events too easily.
现代占星学中解释恶的另一种进路，是把事件视为「演化的必要」：遭受的恶是灵魂继续成长演化所必需的，以某种方式说，它对你有益。
Another approach to explaining evil that you will find in some modern astrology, is to view an event as 'evolutionary necessity', meaning that evil suffered is necessary for continued soul growth and evolution, that in some way it is good for you.
若相信「你创造自己的现实」这套心念控制理论，那么按定义，发生在你身上的一切都是自己的错；你在事件中看到自己的思想被映回来。我们考察过的几位现代占星师明白说过这一点，丽兹·格林和伊莎贝尔·希基尤其如此。它是许多现代新时代式灵性背后非常常见的信念。
If you subscribe to the mind control theory that says you create your own reality, then anything that happens to you is by definition your own fault, and you are seeing your own thoughts mirrored back to you in events. We saw this explicitly stated in the words of several of the modern astrologers we looked at, most notably Liz Greene and Isabel Hickey. It is a very common belief underlying much modern, new age style spirituality.
还有一种在今天颇有吸引力、源于东方哲学的进路：一切表面上的恶事都只是摩耶（幻相）Maya，只是表象与幻觉；在表象背后存在不变的永恒善。这条思路在现代占星学中也很强，与心念创造说一样；两种进路经常组合出现。
Yet another approach that has some appeal today that has roots in eastern philosophy is to say that all apparent evil events are just Maya, appearance, illusion, and that behind this appearance there is an unchanging eternal good. This streak is quite also strong in modern astrology as is the mind creation approach, and I think it is common to see a combination of these two approaches together.
另一种进路诉诸命运Fortune，也就是事情就是会发生：一个枪手走进房间开火时，你碰巧在场。这只是坏运。它没有真正解释或证明任何事为正当；但好处是，不会因为坏事发生在你身上而责怪你。这种进路只说恶事可以发生在好人身上，到此为止。乍看之下，它最不令人满意——丽兹·格林直斥其毫无用处——但我越思考，就越认为，承认命运因素而不归责，非常忠实于人类经验。在真实咨询工作脉络中，我发现它十分实际有用，因为能避开把坏事怪罪于当事人的陷阱。处理伤害已经够糟，无须再往上叠加一层罪疚重担。
Another approach is to appeal to Fortune, which is just that things happen. You happened to be in the room when a gunman walked in and opened fire. This is just bad luck. It doesn't really explain or justify anything, but it has the advantage of not blaming you for something bad happening to you. This approach says that evil things can happen to good people, and it stops there. At first glance this seems the least satisfactory - Liz Greene flat out called it useless - but the more I think of it, the more I think that acknowledging an element of Fortune without assigning blame is very true to human experience. It is an approach that I find can be very practical and useful in the context of actual client work, as it avoids the trap of blaming the person for anything bad that happens to them. Dealing with harm is bad enough without an extra burden of guilt tacked on top of that.
还可以宣称万事最终都共同成就善，以此为恶辩护。这个说法出现在基督教《新约》保罗书信中：「万事都互相效力，叫爱神的人得益处，就是按他旨意被召的人。」若生活在相信仁慈造物主的脉络中，这是令人满意的解释；但在该框架之外，它可能听来只是陈词。它实际上什么也没有解释，至多是一句安慰，保证不知怎的，一切最终都会得到最好结局。除非我知道来访者相信这样的上帝，否则不会对他使用这种说法；即便他相信，我也会极其谨慎。原书 p.184
Evil can also be justified by claiming that everything works together for good in the end. This appears in the Christian New Testament, in the letters of Paul - Everything works together for good, for those who love God, according to his purpose. That is a satisfying explanation if you live in the context of belief in a benevolent creator God, but it can sound like a platitude if you are outside of that framework. It really doesn't explain anything, and at best it is a statement of reassurance that, somehow, all will work out for the best. I would not use that with a client unless I knew they believed in such a God, and even there I would tread very carefully.
出处《罗马书》8:28
中文采用和合本完整句。原书英语是意译式摘述，没有把经文当作对受害者即时说出的标准答案；作者反而强调，脱离信仰脉络强加这句话，可能沦为空洞安慰甚至二次伤害。
解释恶还有一个更大框架：诉诸天意Providence，主张世界中存在一种能够从恶中带出善的力量与秩序。它与「万事共同成就善」并不完全相同；后一句太像在说恶本来就是为了产出善而纳入计划。天意所说的是：即便恶事发生，宇宙中仍有力量能从中带出善。我个人觉得这个框架最令人满意；但在来访者解读中，我仍会依对方自身信念与态度极谨慎地使用。
Another larger framework for explaining evil is to appeal to Providence, to claim that there is a power and order in the world that can bring good out of evil. This is not quite the same as saying everything works together for good, which sounds too much like saying that evil was part of a plan to bring good from it. Providence says that, even if evil events happen, there is a power in the universe that can bring good out of it. This is the framework that I personally find the most satisfying, but again I would use it with great caution in client readings depending on the person's own beliefs and attitude.
无论偏爱上述哪一种解释，我认为都有一种反应值得采用。坏事、凶性之事，甚至恶事发生在你身上时，你可能很少或完全无法控制。承认这一点，是妥善面对恶事的第一步。你确实拥有控制与选择的领域，是如何回应。接下来往哪里走？如何选择自己的反应？聚焦这一点永远有价值。
There is reaction that I think is useful regardless of which of these explanations you favor. When something bad, something malefic, even something evil, happens to you, you may have little or no control over that. Admitting that is a first step to dealing well with an evil event. The one area where you do have control and choice is in how you respond. Where do you go from here, and how can you choose your reaction? That is always worthwhile to focus on.
我想在这里应用切斯特顿检验，用简单话重述这些解释，鲜明直白地迫使我们留意：对一个真实例子来说，各种恶的解释究竟会产生什么效果。
I want to use the Chesterton Test here, restating these in simple words, to really, starkly get us to pay attention to how these various explanations of evil might work out in an actual example.
加布里埃尔·吉福兹Gabrielle Giffords是美国政治人物，曾任众议员。2011 年 1 月 8 日，吉福兹议员成为刺杀目标，后脑近距离中枪。这在该词的两重意义上都是恶：既造成极端伤害，也来自蓄意伤害的意图。
Gabrielle Giffords is an American politician who was a member of the House of Representatives. On January 8, 2011 congresswoman Giffords was the victim of an assassination attempt and was shot point blank in the back of the head. This is evil in both senses of the term; it is extreme harm, and it came from a deliberate intent to harm.
我想把这些面对恶的进路逐一改用简单直白的话重述。请设想在事件刚发生、情绪最炽烈的时刻听见下面每一种回应。我把话写成第一人称；你也可以想象有人对站在妻子身边的丈夫马克·凯利说这些话，或在她恢复意识、开始康复后对她本人说。原书 p.185
I want to take each of these approaches to dealing with evil and restate them simply and bluntly. Consider each of these responses to that event, in the heat of the moment. I phrase these as first person, but you can also imagine something like these words being said to her husband Mike Kelly standing there next to his wife. Or, you can imagine these being spoken to her after she became conscious again and began to recover.
校记Mike Kelly 应为 Mark Kelly
吉福兹的丈夫是马克·凯利（Mark Kelly），原书本页误作 Mike，后文第 187 页又正确写作 Mark Kelly。中文径改为「马克·凯利」，英文栏保留原书误名以便核对。
业力——「你的头部中枪了。这是你自己的错。」
Karma - "You were shot in the head. It was your own fault."
创造自己的现实——「你的头部中枪了。是你的负面思想造成的。」
Create your own Reality - "You were shot in the head. You caused it by your negative thoughts."
摩耶——「你的头部中枪了。这不是真的。真实的你不会受到伤害。」
Maya - "You were shot in the head. This is not real. The real you cannot be harmed."
演化的必要——「你的头部中枪了。你需要它来成长，它对你有益。」
Evolutionary Necessity - "You were shot in the head. You need this to grow, it is good for you."
万事最终导向善——「你的头部中枪了。一切最后都会得到最好的结局。」
All things lead to good in the end - "You were shot in the head. It will all work out for the best."
天意——「你的头部中枪了。上帝能从中带出善。」
Providence - "You were shot in the head. God can bring good out of this."
命运——「你的头部中枪了。这不是你的错；坏事就是会发生。」
Fortune - "You were shot in the head. This is not your fault; bad things happen."
在事件刚发生的炽烈时刻，上述答案中，我在情感上唯一稍能接受的是命运。只有这项解释不试图解释、不提供理由；它只承认坏事已经发生，当事人不该受责怪。其他每种回应都在尝试解释不可解释之物、证明不可辩护之事为正当、思想不可思议之事。
The only one of these answers that I find at all emotionally acceptable in the heat of the moment is that of Fortune. It is the one explanation that does not attempt to explain or to give a reason, it just acknowledges that something bad happened and the person is not to blame. Every one of the other responses is attempting to explain the unexplainable, to justify the unjustifiable, to think of the unthinkable.
恶，真正的道德之恶，是一种侵犯、一项矛盾，是绝不应该发生之事。然而它确实发生。我们必须从这里开始。原书 p.186
Evil, real moral evil, is a violation, a contradiction, something that should not ever happen. But it does. We have to start there.
宗教与神学史上有几种论证上帝存在的经典论证，也有反对上帝存在的论证。我若认真思考其中一些支持上帝存在的论证，会觉得非常有说服力。
In the history of religion and theology there are several classic arguments for the existence of God, and there are also arguments against God's existence. I find some of the arguments for God's existence to be very compelling if I think them through.
从另一边来说，对我而言，反对一位良善造物主存在的论证里，真正有分量的只有一个：世上存在道德之恶。既然上帝是善的、创造也是善的，如何解释道德之恶存在？面对一个真实恶事，我不知道有什么论证听起来不只是搬弄词句。
Coming at it from the other side, as far as I am concerned there is really only one argument against the existence of a good creator God which has any real weight, and that is the existence of moral evil in the world. Given that God is good and creation is good, how do you explain the existence of moral evil? Faced with an actual evil event I do not know of any argument that doesn't sound like mere juggling of words.
任何面对恶的进路，都必须先正面凝视它赤裸的恐怖与矛盾。我们不能把它解释掉；在做任何其他事情之前，必须朴素地接受事实。「你的头部中枪了。我在这里陪你。」或者更好，什么也不说，只抱住对方，只在那里陪着。我们必须从赤裸朴素的接纳开始，才能进一步给予同情与援助。
Any approach to evil has to begin by squarely facing the stark horror and contradiction of it. We can't explain it away. We have to have plain acceptance before we can do anything else. "You were shot in the head. I am here with you." Or, better yet, just hold them. Just be there. We need to start with plain naked acceptance before we can extend sympathy or aid.
人有自由意志，有选择能力；这必然包括选择恶的自由。因为我们在地上彼此互动，任何人都可能成为他人恶行的承受者。面对「恶的存在反驳上帝存在」这一论证，我唯一想得出的回答是：人类自由意志之善，超过与它必然同来的恶之可能。
Humans have free will, the ability to choose. That necessarily includes the freedom to choose evil. Since we interact here on earth this means that any person can be on the receiving end of someone else's evil act. The only way I can think of to answer the argument against God's existence because of evil is to argue that the good of human free will outweighs the possibility of evil that necessarily comes with it.
这又引向一个更高层次的问题：既然恶行可能被选择、可能发生，宇宙秩序是否以这样一种方式运作，使善能从恶中产生？
This leads to a higher level question. Given that evil acts can be chosen and can happen, does the order of the universe work in such a way that good can come out of that?
善能从恶中产生吗？
Can good come out of evil?
我个人深信可以。我认为前面提到的同一可怕例子——美国众议员加布里埃尔·吉福兹遭枪击——为此提供了很好的论据。
I am personally convinced that it can. I think the same horrible example that referred to earlier, the shooting of American Congresswoman Gabrielle Giffords, is a very good argument for that.
我认为她如今正在做重要而有意义的工作，也钦佩她和丈夫马克·凯利的力量与正直。原书 p.187
I think she is doing important and meaningful work now, and I admire the strength and integrity of both her and her husband Mark Kelly.
最终，对于恶，我个人唯一觉得稍能满意的回答是相信天意：宇宙中有某种底层力量，甚至能从最坏的恶中带出善。这也见证人类精神中某种强大而有复原力之物，能够这样回应；我认为最终必须把它追溯到创造并维持人类精神的力量。
Ultimately, the only answer to evil that I personally find satisfying at all is the belief in Providence, that there is some underlying power in our universe that is able to bring good even out of the worst evil. This is also a testimony to something powerful and resilient in the human spirit that can respond in this way, and ultimately I think we have to trace that to the power which creates and sustains the human spirit.
这并不是说恶从一开始就是善，不是说它属于上帝计划，也不是把它解释掉，或说从长远看万事都会得到最好结局。它更像某种奇迹般的炼金术或转化。恶并未被计划，却能被转化，结出善果。
This is not making the evil good in the first place, or saying that it is part of God's plan, or explaining it away, or saying that everything works out for the best in the long run. It is more like a miraculous kind of alchemy or transformation. Evil is not planned, but it can be transformed to bear good fruit.
终有一天。
Eventually.
但即便我相信天意，也觉得不能把这种信念强加给任何人。我认为，每个人都必须在自己灵魂的私密与独处中面对这个终极问题，作出自己的决定。
But even with my belief in Providence, I do not feel I can push that on anyone else. I think that each individual person has to face that ultimate question in the privacy and solitude of their own soul and come to their own decision.
面对一个正在承受恶行巨大伤害的人，我不会一开始就谈上帝的天意。若他不分享我的信仰，强加给他就是冒犯；那很容易让人觉得我在把恶解释掉。面对真实的恶，我们所能做的最好事情，是尽力找到勇气与正直，不靠解释消解，直面它赤裸的恐怖；同时准备好以一切可能方式给予爱与援助。
Faced with a person who is dealing with the great harm of an evil action, I would not start speaking of God's Providence. If they do not share my faith there, it would be an affront to push it on them. It could easily feel too much like explaining it away. Faced with actual evil, the best we can do is to try to find the courage and integrity to face the stark horror of it without explaining it away. That, and be ready to offer love and aid in whatever way we can.
提示“命运”与“天意”在不同时间发挥作用
事件刚发生时，作者只接受不归责的「命运」：承认坏事发生，先陪伴援助。较长时间后，他个人才以「天意」理解善可能从恶中结出。把第二层答案抢先塞进第一时刻，就会从希望变成解释与强迫。

恶与转化Evil and Transformation

面对恶时，有时还会发生另一种有趣现象。请想想下面这些例子。
There is another interesting phenomenon that sometimes happens with facing evil. Consider these examples.
犹太心理学家维克多·弗兰克尔Viktor Frankl是第二次世界大战纳粹死亡营的受害者；他幸存下来，最终写成二十世纪伟大著作之一《活出意义来：从集中营说到存在主义》，见证人类精神的复原力与伟大。
There is Victor Frankl, the Jewish psychologist who was a victim of the Nazi death camps during World War Two, who survived and eventually wrote Man's Search for Meaning, one of the great books of the 20th century, a tribute to the resilience and greatness of the human spirit.
费奥多尔·陀思妥耶夫斯基Fyodor Dostoyevsky曾被判死刑，后获缓刑；他由这段经验走向对基督教上帝的信仰。原书 p.188
There is Fyodor Dostoyevsky, who came to his belief in the Christian God from the experience of being sentenced to death, and then reprieved.
亚历山大·索尔仁尼琴Aleksandr Solzhenitsyn经历苏联劳改营，后来写成《古拉格群岛》。索尔仁尼琴是东正教基督徒。
There is Alexandr Solzhenitzyn, who experienced the Russian labor camps and later wrote The Gulag Archipelago. Solzhenitzyn is East Orthodox Christian.
埃及前总统安瓦尔·萨达特Anwar Sadat曾是暴力激进分子，后来被捕，在可怕条件下遭单独监禁；出狱后，他有了新的信仰感，成为中东地区以非暴力开创和平的人，直到遇刺身亡。
There is Anwar Sadat, former President of Egypt, who was a violent radical until he experienced being arrested and thrown in solitary confinement in horrific conditions - and then emerged with a new sense of faith, and became a non-violent pioneer for peace in the Middle East until he was assassinated.
当代还有乔丹·彼得森Jordan Peterson；他说，自己的工作之所以致力于人类精神之善，是因为曾直面真实之恶的存在，也直面这样一种空洞恐怖：如果世界在恶那里止步、不存在任何善的力量，会变成什么。
In our current day there is Jordan Peterson, who credits the motivation for his work on the goodness of the human spirit to the experience of coming face to face with the existence of real evil, and the empty horror if the world stopped there and there was no force for good.
校记两个人名的英文拼写
原书作 Victor Frankl 与 Alexandr Solzhenitzyn；通行拼法分别为 Viktor Frankl、Aleksandr Solzhenitsyn。中文按通行姓名「维克多·弗兰克尔」「亚历山大·索尔仁尼琴」，英文栏保留原书。
这些人全都直面赤裸的道德之恶，经历它鲜明而不加遮蔽的现实——也全都带着对终极善的强烈信念走了出来。
All of these people came face to face with the raw, stark experience and realization of naked moral evil - and all emerged with a strong faith in ultimate good.
我们在这里面对一个奥秘。我没有走过那条路，所以只能指向它、承认它，不试图解释。有时，我们必须凝视人类之恶的深渊，才能真正理解「善」意味着什么。
We are face to face with a mystery here. I have not walked that path, so I can only point to it and acknowledge it without attempting to explain it. Sometimes we need to stare into the abyss of human Evil in order to really understand what The Good means.

命运Fortune

我想以 C. S. 刘易斯C. S. Lewis《被弃的意象：中世纪与文艺复兴文学导论》中的一段长引文，开启这篇关于命运的文章。这是一本极好的书，让人感受到生活在传统世界中可能是什么样子——在那里，占星学的诸天球是自然世界秩序中理所当然的一部分。刘易斯提到的但丁引文出自《神曲》。原书 p.189
I want to begin this essay on Fortune with a long quote from C S Lewis from a book called The Discarded Image: An Introduction to Medieval and Renaissance Literature. This is an excellent book to give a feel for what it must have been like to live within the traditional world where the spheres of astrology were a given part of the natural world order. The Dante quote Lewis refers to is from the Commedia.
我们已经看到，月亮以下的一切都可变而偶然。我们也看到，每一个天球都由一种智性引导。地球不运动，因此无须引导，所以人们通常认为不必为她配属一种智性。
We have already seen that all below the Moon is mutable and contingent. We have also seen that each of the celestial spheres is guided by an Intelligence. Since Earth does not move and therefore needs no guidance, it was not generally felt that an Intelligence need be assigned to her.
据我所知，是但丁率先提出那个精彩构想：地球毕竟也有一位智性，而这位尘世智性不是别的，正是命运。诚然，命运并不驾驭地球沿轨道运行；她以适合一个静止球体的方式，履行智性的职分。
It was left, so far as I know, for Dante to make the brilliant suggestion that she has one after all, and that this terrestrial Intelligence is none other than Fortune. Fortune, to be sure, does not steer the Earth through an orbit; she fulfills the office of an Intelligence in the mode proper to a stationary globe.
但丁说，上帝为诸天配备引导者，「使每一部分都把光辉传给其他部分，公平地分配光明」；同样，他也为尘世的荣华设立了一位总管与引导者，使这些骗人的福分不时从一个民族或家族转移到另一个，而人的智慧无法阻止。因此，一个民族统治之时，另一个便渐趋衰弱。
God, says Dante, who gave the heavens their guides 'so that every part communicates splendour to every other, equitably distributing light, likewise ordained a general minister and guide to worldly splendours; one who should from time to time transfer these deceptive benefits from one nation or stock to another in a fashion which no human wisdom can prevent. That is why one people rules while another grows weak.'
因此，凡人的舌头对她多有辱骂，「但她有福，丝毫听不见。在其他原初受造物之间，她幸福地转动自己的天球，为自身的福乐而欢欣。」
For this she is much abused by mortal tongues, 'but she is blessed and never hears them. Happy among the other primal creatures, she turns her sphere and rejoices in her bliss.'
……既然尘世荣华本是骗人的，让它们流转就很合宜。池水必须不断搅动，否则就会腐臭。搅动池水的天使以此行动为乐，正如诸天球以自身运行而欢欣。原书 p.190
...Since worldly splendours are deceptive, it is fit that they should circulate. The pond must be continually stirred or it will become pestilential. The angel who stirs it rejoices in this action as the heavenly spheres rejoice in theirs.
帝国的兴衰不取决于其功过，也不取决于人类总体演化的某种「趋势」，只取决于命运粗粝的正义——让每一方轮流登场；这一观念并未随中世纪一同消逝。
The conception that the rise and fall of empires depends not on desert, nor on any 'trend' in the total evolution of humanity, but simply on the rough justice of Fortune, giving all their turns, did not pass away with the Middle Ages.
背景命运作为地球的智性
刘易斯在这里转述但丁《神曲·地狱篇》第七歌。命运不是盲目的偶然，也不是个人应得的奖惩，而是负责让尘世福分循环转移的宇宙职分；她的「粗粝正义」在于人人轮流，而不在于逐案按功过分配。
这是一段内涵极丰富的话，与我们现代思考世界的方式很不一样。我认为它提出了几个很值得思索的问题，下面想从两个不同方向接近它。
This is a very rich passage, one that is very different from our modern way of thinking about the world. I think it raises some very worthwhile points, and I want to approach it here from a couple of different directions.
刘易斯在这里描述的命运模型，符合传统世界模型：失序的领域位于月亮轨道以内，是月下世界、四元素的世界。这个天球之外，是古典七曜的领域，那里运动极其规则而可预测。我们拥有行星过去与未来数千年的星历；相较之下，地上的世界却是一团失序、不可预测的混乱。我们能提前数千年预测行星位置，却无法准确预测几天后的天气。
The model of fortune which Lewis describes here fits in the traditional world model where the disordered area is within the moon's orbit, the sublunary world, the world of the four elements. Outside of that sphere is the realm of the seven classical planets where the movement is very regular and predictable. We have ephemerides of the planetary positions for thousands of years into the past and future, and by comparison the world here on earth is disorderly and unpredictable mess. We can predict the positions of planets thousands of years in advance, and we can't accurately predict the weather a few days out.
在这个模型中，命运的隐含目的之一，是帮助我们认识到：在这个变易世界里，表象具有欺骗性。命运起落的目的，是教我们什么才真正有价值。按此模型，以通常尘世意义来说，好运或坏运不是功过问题，而是运气问题。这与我们这个崇尚成就的社会完全背道而驰。命运轮流转动，这一观念与周期必有高低起伏相关。我们需要两者都经历过，才能拥有完整的人生。
Part of the implicit purpose of Fortune in this model is to help us realize that appearances are deceptive down here in the mutable world. The purpose of changes of fortune is to teach what is really valuable. In this model good or bad fortune in the usual worldly sense is not a matter of merit but of luck. That runs totally against the grain of our achievement-oriented society. Fortune takes turns, a concept related to cycles having ups and downs. We need to experience both to have full human lives.
这意味着，生命的目的与好运或坏运毫无关系。最终，我们不能把幸运人生归功于自己，也不能因不幸人生而责怪自己。我们是一个更大秩序的一部分；从这个意义说，好运与坏运都超出我们的控制。
This means that the purpose of life is not related to good or bad fortune in any way. This also means that ultimately we cannot take credit for having fortunate lives, nor can we take blame for having unfortunate lives. We are part of a larger order, and good or bad fortune in that sense is beyond our control.
传统占星学与现代占星学在这里有着截然不同的目的。原书 p.191
This is a place where traditional and modern astrology have distinctly different purposes.
我们已经看到，二十世纪占星学带有强烈的道德倾向，传递的信息是「你得到的是自己应得的」。传统占星学并不采取这种进路。在传统占星学中，尊贵与弱势的评估工具显示命运当下趋向何方：这一时期是好是坏，是轻松幸运，还是艰难不幸。在通常的传统语境里，谈及好运与坏运时，根本不会提出道德目的的问题。在传统占星学与传统宇宙模型中，好运坏运都会降临在好人和坏人身上。
We have seen that there is a strong moral streak in 20th century astrology, the message that you get what you deserve. Traditional astrology does not take that approach. In traditional astrology the evaluation tools of dignity and debility tell which way Fortune is trending at the moment, if this will be a good or bad time, easy and fortunate, or difficult and unfortunate. The whole question of moral purpose doesn't come up in the usual traditional context relative to good and bad fortune. In traditional astrology, and in the traditional model of the cosmos, good and bad fortune happen to both good and bad people.
提示吉凶不是道德判决
传统技法用「尊贵／弱势」「吉星／凶星」描述事件较顺遂还是较艰难，并不据此宣判一个人的善恶或功过。把坏运解释成「你应得的」，正是作者要从咨询中排除的二次伤害。
现代占星学的核心部分之一是处理目的问题，因此要让命运概念发挥作用，我们就需要思考它如何纳入一个更大的目的。正如但丁的引文所示，我们必须先考虑什么不是人生目的——这会排除许多最初可能想当然认定的目的。
Since a central part of modern astrology is addressing the issue of purpose, for the concept of Fortune to be useful we need to think how it would fit in a larger purpose. As the quote from Dante shows, we need to start by considering what is not the purpose of human life - and that eliminates a lot of the purposes we might first assume.
生命的目的不是成功、获胜、富有、拥有财富或力量，也不是具备任何其他外在品质。生命的目的不是精通随心所欲的艺术，不是实现全部潜能，也不是把自己的愿景强加于世界。生命的目的不是高度演化或开悟。我列出的每一种目的，根本上都是精英主义的，因为它们只适用于少数获选之人。只要稍加思索便会看出：若把其中任何目标当作生命的真正目的，整部历史中很大一部分人的生命就会因此变得价值较低，甚至毫无价值。
The purpose of life is not to succeed, or to win, or to be rich, or to be wealthy, or strong, or to have any other external quality. The purpose of life is not the fine art of doing whatever you choose, or manifesting your full potential, or imposing your vision on the world. The purpose of life is not to be highly evolved or enlightened. Every one of these purposes I listed are at root elitist, as they can apply only to a chosen few. Just a moment's thought should show that any of those goals as the true purpose of life renders the lives of a large proportion of people throughout history either less valuable or worthless.
如果你说生命的目的在于富有，那么你就把穷人的生命价值贬成了零。
If you say the purpose of life is to be wealthy then you have reduced the value of poor people's lives to nothing.
如果生命的目的在于健康，那么你就必须解释那些一生都在与疾病或虚弱抗争之人的生命。
If the purpose of life is to be healthy, then you have to make sense of the lives of people who battle with sickness or weakness all their lives.
如果生命的目的在于长寿，那么你就需要解释那些英年早逝者的生命。原书 p.192
If the purpose of life is to live long, then you need to explain the lives of people who die young.
如果生命的目的在于灵性演化或觉醒，那么穷困而未受教育的人又怎么办？他们只能把时间精力用于辛劳糊口，根本没有余暇享受灵性开悟这项奢侈。
If the purpose of life is to be spiritually evolved or awakened, where does this leave the lives of poor and uneducated people who have time and energy only to work hard to make ends meet, and no time for the luxury of spiritual enlightenment.
如果所有人的生命都有价值，那么人生目的就不能系于任何形式的好运或坏运。
If all human lives have worth then the purpose of human life cannot be tied to any form of good or bad fortune.
传统还提供了另一种可以在这里考虑的进路，它与我们现代所说演化与进步一路向前的观念相抵触。
There is another approach from the tradition that we can consider here, one that runs counter to our modern notion of the forward march of evolution and progress.
我们曾提到柏拉图《理想国》第十卷的厄尔神话。本书附录有一篇长文考察那一章。若读过其中人们选择自己想要的生命之签的描述，就很清楚第一印象可能骗人。看似财富与权力兼具的人生，会带来通向暴政、贪婪与残酷的后果；看似艰辛挣扎的人生，最终却令人满足，并能建立德性。
We mentioned Plato's myth of Er in book 10 of The Republic. That chapter is examined in a long essay in the Appendix of this book. If you read the description there of people choosing the Lot of the life they want, it is clear that first appearances can be deceptive. What looks like a life of wealth and power has consequences leading to tyranny, greed and cruelty. What looks like a life of hardship and struggle ends up being satisfying and builds virtue.
柏拉图在故事中还指出，各种生命通常倾向于交替出现。刚结束两世之间受奖赏时期的人，很可能选择轻松人生，但那会带来艰难后果，要求在死后受罚；刚结束受罚时期的人，则更可能选择艰难或卑微的人生，而它将结出善果，最终获得奖赏。在这个模型中，人们一般应对艰难人生，反而比应对正面轻松的人生做得更好。
Plato also points out in his story that the lives generally tend to alternate. People who are just off of a period where they were rewarded between lives are likely to choose an easy life, with hard consequences calling for punishment after that life. People just off of a period of punishment are more likely to choose a difficult or humble life that will have good fruit and end up being rewarded. In this model people as a general rule do better with the difficult lives than with the positive and easy ones.
这个模型并不存在一世又一世之间直线式的进步与成长，而是来回弹荡，上上下下。
This model does not have straight line progress and growth between lives. It bounces, back and forth, up and down.
我们在这里看到的是另一种价值标准。在刚才考察的可能人生目标清单里——财富、权力、好运等等——每一个目标都依赖某种外在实现。在柏拉图模型中，衡量一生价值的唯一真正尺度是德性。
We are looking at a different sort of standard of values here. In the list of possible life goals we considered - wealth, power, fortune and so on - all of the goals depend on some external fulfillment. In the Platonic model the only real measure of the worth of a life is in terms of virtue.
无论人生的外在环境如何，重要的问题都是：一个人是否始终选择以有德性的方式行动。在柏拉图的神话里，带来奖赏期或惩罚期的正是这些标准。原书 p.193
Regardless of the external circumstances of life, the important question is whether a person chooses to consistently act in virtuous ways. Those are the criteria that result in the periods of reward or punishment in the Platonic myth.
我认为，许多现代人难以应对命运观念，部分原因在于现代世界中我们对人生目的的理解已经改变太多。在西方富裕社会里，我们已经习惯认为自己配得好运。媒体极力鼓励这种价值观，因为它是推销商品的绝佳动力：去争取吧，你值得拥有。
I think that part of the reason that many modern people find the notion of Fortune hard to deal with is that our conception of the purpose of human life has changed so very much in the modern world. In our western affluent society we have gotten used to the idea that we deserve good fortune. The media goes a long way towards encouraging that value, as it is a very good motivator to sell things. Go for it, you deserve it.
如果在现代世界里，生命的目的并不是以任意一种方式取得成功——富有、强壮、美丽、著名或聪明——那么它究竟是什么？人能否生来贫穷、虚弱或畸形，一生挣扎，依然拥有有价值、值得活的人生？想想你认识的、生命符合这番描述的人——最好是你关爱或钦佩的人——再问自己：你钦佩的究竟是什么？
If the purpose of life in our modern world isn't to be successful in any number of ways - rich or strong or beautiful or famous or intelligent - then what is? Can people be born poor, or weak, or deformed, struggle all their lives, and still have lives that have value, that are worth living? Think of the people you know whose lives fit this description - preferably people you care for or admire - and ask yourself: what is it you admire?
我的父母来自纽约市布鲁克林的工人阶级，具有意大利与爱尔兰天主教血统。他们成长的年代存在严重的反天主教偏见，商店会挂出这样的牌子：「招聘帮工：爱尔兰人免问。」
My parents were working class, Brooklyn, New York City, of Italian and Irish Catholic ancestry, growing up in an era of serious anti-Catholic bias when shops would display signs saying, Help Wanted: No Irish Need Apply.
母亲是缝纫机操作工，父亲是簿记员；第二次世界大战期间，他想参军，却因视力不佳未能如愿。他起初试图背下视力表来通过入伍体检，但战争爆发后复检时还是失败了。
My mom was a sewing machine operator, my dad was a book-keeper who wanted to join the army during World War Two but was prevented by poor vision. He originally tried memorizing the eye chart to get into the army, but failed on a re-exam when war broke out.
父母两人都非常勤劳，生活都极其节俭，竭尽所能给我和妹妹良好的生活。妹妹十几岁显出严重精神疾病的征兆后，他们把后半生都用来尽自己所能照顾她。
Both of my parents worked very hard, both lived very frugally, both did all they could to give my sister and I a good life. When my sister developed signs of serious mental illness in her teens, both my parents dedicated the latter half of their lives to taking care of her the best they could.
两人都聪明，却不是才华横溢。谁都没有高中毕业；他们没有那样的机会。他们并非完美的父母——我继承了他们的许多问题——但他们把自己能给的都给了我和妹妹。原书 p.194
Both were intelligent but not brilliant. Neither graduated high school; neither had the chance. They were not perfect parents - I inherited a lot of their problems - but they gave my sister and I what they could.
我把他们看作平凡的圣徒，或善良正派的人。而且我不认为像我父母这样的人罕见。
I think of them as ordinary saints, or good decent people. And, I do not think that people like my parents are uncommon.
无论我父母拥有的那种东西是什么，它就是使人的生命有价值之物，与好运或坏运毫无关系，也与他们在灵性上演化到何等程度无关。两人承受的坏运与苦难都超过应有份额，但他们仍是好人——或是不顾这些而为善，或是因这些而为善。如今我倾向后一种想法：他们的挑战，正是使他们成为那样好人的一部分。
Whatever it is that my parents had is what makes human life valuable, and it has nothing to do with good or bad fortune. It also has nothing to do with how spiritually evolved they were. Both had more than their share of bad fortune and suffering, and they were good people, either despite that, or because of that. I now lean towards the latter way of thinking, that their challenges are part of what made them the good people they were.
年岁越长，我越珍视只做一个善良、正派、关怀他人的普通人，也越明白这其实有多么困难。
The older I get, the more I value simply being a good, decent caring ordinary person, and the more I realize how difficult that really is.
现代占星学中有一股精英主义倾向。我在这里刻意不列出具体名字，因为我的批评很尖锐，也想让描述足够模糊，无法追溯到具体人物。当你说星辰支配普通而无觉知的大众之行动，但只要你有足够觉知，就有能力选择发生在自己身上的事，这股精英主义便隐含其中。当然，占星师及其受众并不属于那群无觉知的大众；他们属于先进者、演化者、灵性觉知者。
There is a streak of modern astrology that is elitist. Here I am deliberately not giving specific names because I am being very critical, and I am trying to make the description vague enough that it can't be tracked back. That elitist streak is implicitly there when you say that the stars govern the actions of the mass of ordinary and unaware people, but that if you aware enough you have the ability to choose what happens to you. And of course, the astrologer and their audience are not among that mass of unaware people, they are among the advanced, the evolved, the spiritually aware.
你还会看见一种未明说的假设：占星师及其受众站在人类灵性演化的最前沿，在无觉知的黑暗中点亮一盏灯，为未开悟的大众引领前路。我听过一位占星师把这种未演化者称为「牲口」，台下随即响起居高临下的笑声。我们何其幸运，竟已经演化得超越他们！
You will also see the unstated assumption that the astrologer and their audience are at the cutting edge of the spiritual evolution of humanity, lighting a lamp in the darkness of unawareness and leading a path forward for the unenlightened masses. I have heard an astrologer refer to such unevolved people as cattle, a comment followed by condescending laughter among the crowd. How fortunate we are to be evolved past that!
如果人生目的在于灵性演化，那么演化程度更高的人就过着价值更高的人生。结论自然随前提而来。原书 p.195
If the purpose of human life is spiritual evolution, then people who are more evolved lead more valuable lives. The conclusion follows the premise.
在我看来，这错得可怕。
That feels horribly wrong to me.
这里有一件我正努力触及的非常重要的事。无论是什么使人的生命值得一过，都不能只局限于富人、名人、成功者、强者或任何类似人群。它必须是普通人生的一部分，是任何人的生命都能具有的品质——无论贫富、知名与否、健康或患病。它不能是一种只限少数人的精英品质。
There is something very important I am getting at here. Whatever it is that makes a human life worth living cannot be confined only to the rich, or the famous or successful or strong or whatever. It needs to be some quality that is part of ordinary human life, any human life, rich or poor, known or unknown, healthy or ill. It cannot be something elitist that is confined to the few.
人生并非全都关乎成功。我能为生命中重要之物找到的最佳词语，是品格与德性。
Human life is not all about success. The best words I can find for what is important in life are character and virtue.
世界上许多灵性传统都提到死亡时的审判：每个人都要面对自己一生如何度过所产生的后果。柏拉图那里有，基督教与犹太教的《圣经》里有，伊斯兰教里有，印度教的轮回模型里也有。
Many world spiritual traditions refer to a time of judgment at death, where each person faces the consequences of how their life was lived. It is in Plato, it is in the Bible in Christianity and Judaism, it is in Islam, and also in the Hindu model of reincarnation.
死亡时接受审判的究竟是什么？无论它是什么，正是同一种东西，能使一个贫穷、辛劳而短暂的人生，依然成为值得经历的美好事物，具有无限价值。
What is being judged at death? Whatever it is, it is the same thing that can make a life that is poor and hard working and short still be a worthwhile thing of beauty and infinite value.
命运在整个人生系统中扮演的角色，其部分目的，是教会我们什么不重要、什么才重要。有一条值得奉行的法则，能让人生无论遭到命运怎样抛掷，都仍然值得。恰恰这一点，可能就是命运在整体秩序中的部分目的。
Part of the purpose of Fortune, the role it plays in the whole system of human life, is to teach us what is not important and what is. There is a law worth living by that makes life worthwhile regardless of what Fortune throws at you. Precisely that may be part of the purpose of Fortune in the overall order.
我们生活在一个更大的秩序之内；当我们找到对这一秩序的感受，并能在其中生活时，就会更幸福、更充实，也更具人性。命运提醒我们这一点。当骄傲与野心开始占据上风，我们以为发生在自己身上的一切好事都是应得之时，命运便会踹我们一脚——假如我们走运的话。
We live within a larger order, and we are happier and more fulfilled, more human, when we find a sense of that order and can live within it. Fortune reminds us of that. When our pride and ambition starts to get the better of us, and we think that we deserve all the good things that happen to us, that is when Fortune kicks us in the butt - if we're lucky.
命运把我们举高，也使我们降卑。被压低、被摔下，就是受辱、变得谦卑；这两个英文词有着相同的词根意义。谦卑是一种特别属于土星的德性，也是所有德性中最美的一种。
We are raised up by Fortune, and we are brought low. To be brought low, to be cast down, is to be humiliated, to be humbled; the two words have the same root meaning. Humility is a peculiarly Saturnian virtue, and one of the most beautiful virtues of all.
背景humiliation 与 humility
两词都经拉丁语 humilis（低下、贴近地面）关联于 humus（土地）。作者借此把「被命运压低」与土星式谦卑连在一起：受辱是外在降卑，谦卑则是由此可能长成的德性。
这种对命运的理解，也为我们运用吉星与凶星概念提供了框架，因为它们关系到命运的高低起伏。我们需要越过那种「只应该发生幸运之事」的想法。在我们这个变易的月下世界里，生活并不如此运作；若所有人只经历好运，这个世界也不会很好地服务于我们。身为人，我们需要好与坏、幸运与不幸、艰难与轻松的混合，也会从这种混合中受益。人生处于周期之中，我们需要周期的所有阶段。原书 p.196
This understanding of Fortune also gives us a framework for working with the concepts of benefic and malefic, which is related to the ups and downs of fortune. We need to get past the idea that only fortunate things are supposed to happen. That is not how life works out down here in our mutable sublunary world, and this world would not serve us well if all experienced only good fortune. As humans we need the mix of good and bad, fortunate and unfortunate, difficult and easy, and we benefit from the mix. Our life is in cycles, and we need all phases of the cycle.
在占星咨询工作中，这种命运观帮助我们摆脱现代一种根深蒂固的看法：你要为自己的人生以及发生在身上的一切负责。那可能造成非常恶劣的心理后果。在应对坏运打击之上，你还得混入责任感、罪疚与责备。人们往往会觉得，当事人必定做过什么才配得此事——无论是在今生还是前世。这样会使本已艰难的处境恶化十倍。
In the area of astrology client work this understanding of Fortune helps us get away from the deep seated modern view that you are responsible for your life, and for everything that happens to you. That can have really nasty psychological effects. On top of dealing with blows of bad fortune you would have mixed in feelings of responsibility, guilt, and blame. There can often be the feeling that the person must have done something to deserve this, either in this life or a previous life. That takes an already difficult situation and makes it ten times worse.
我做过一些来访者咨询，其中的核心目的，就是帮助对方认识到：无论促使他前来咨询的严重事故或疾病是什么，都不是他的错。
I have had client sessions where the central part of the purpose is helping the person realize they were not to blame for whatever kind of serious accident or illness was the core reason for the session.
一旦清除了这层障碍——一旦清除因遭逢坏运而产生的、不必要且有害的罪疚与羞耻——我们就能明晰地处理当事人可以如何选择对处境作出回应。我们未必能控制发生在自己身上的事，但有一件事确实可以选择：希望怎样回应并应对它。这是一条非常有力的斯多葛原则，也是良好的心理与情绪卫生。
Once that is out of the way - once we clear away any unnecessary and harmful feelings of guilt and shame about experiencing bad fortune - then we are clear to address how the person can choose their response to the situation. We may not necessarily control what happens to us, but the one thing we can choose is how we wish to respond and deal with it. That is a very powerful Stoic principle that is also good mental and emotional hygiene.

老年Old Age

在这一章，我想分享自己的人生经验，也谈谈关于衰老我正在学到的一些事。我们的文化几乎没有为如何好好变老提供榜样，所以我不得不自行尝试与探索。我现在六十七岁，把自己看作一个入门级老年人。今年我遇到一些重大的健康问题，使我以一种全新的方式切身感受到自己的年龄与必死性。原书 p.197
In this chapter, I want to share from my own life experience, and touch on some of the things I am learning about aging. Our culture gives me few role models for aging well, so I am having to experiment and explore on my own. I am currently 67 years old, and I think of myself as an entry-level senior citizen. I am having some major health problems this year that have me really feeling my age and my mortality in a new way.
我认为，现代文化通常对待老年的方式非常局限。媒体向我们展示的图景，鼓励我们假装衰老根本不会发生；要不然，就把衰老换一种说法、重新包装，让它听来像青春的延续。
I am think our modern culture's general approach to old age is very limited. The media presents us with pictures that encourage us to pretend that aging just doesn't happen - either that, or rephrase or repackage aging so that it sounds like a continuation of youth.
你感觉多老，才算多老。六十岁是新的四十岁。七十五岁依然年轻。
You're only as old as you feel. 60 is the new 40. 75 years young.
胡扯。
Bull.
我以前订阅过美国退休人员协会American Association of Retired Persons的《AARP 杂志》。里面全是微笑、健康、体格健美而富裕的人物照片；他们站在高尔夫球场上或泳池旁，头发染得鲜亮，步履轻快，已经准备好享受那一切美妙的闲暇时光。
I used to subscribe to AARP magazine (AARP is American Association of Retired Persons), and it was filled with photos of smiling, healthy, physically fit, affluent people, on golf courses or next to pools - color in their hair and a spring in their step, ready to enjoy all of that wonderful leisure time.
我认识的人里，没有谁像那些照片中的人。
I don't know any people like the ones I see in those pictures.
这种否认衰老影响的态度，对女性似乎尤其具有束缚性；社会鼓励她们尽可能长久地保持年轻外貌。《AARP 杂志》里有些关于娱乐行业女性从业者的文章，把这件事推到了既可笑又可怜的地步。如果一个七十多岁的女人仍在努力让自己看起来、行动起来像二十岁出头，那就真有严重的问题。
This attitude of denying the effects of aging seems to be especially limiting for women, who are encouraged to keep their youthful appearance just as long as possible. In AARP some of the articles about women in entertainment careers take that to a point somewhere between funny and pitiful. If a woman in her seventies is still trying to look and act like she is in her early twenties then something is seriously wrong.
这种图景暗示，老年完全关乎「我」——关乎放松和享受自己。这是一种非常以自我为中心的态度。
The picture presented implies that old age is all about me, about relaxing and enjoying myself. It is a very self-centered sort of attitude.
在这一切人工制造、以自我为中心的包装下，我们已经失去这样一种认识：老年本身具有独特价值，有别于青春。我们也完全失去了这种意识：年长者有一种只有年龄才能带来的特殊礼物可以给予。我们需要把它找回来。原书 p.198
With all of this artificial self-centered packaging we have lost the sense of old age itself as having its own particular and unique value, different from youth. We have also have lost all sense of the aged having a particular gift to give that can only come with age. We need to get that back.
这与我们前面讨论的文化失衡相连，符合我们的文化对青春与新事物的崇拜。凡是旧的都被视为过时，应该丢弃；不幸的是，老人往往也包括在内。令人难过的是，老年人经常受到居高临下的说话方式，或者干脆被忽视。年纪越大，你就越来越隐形。我才刚开始体验这一点。
This ties in with our cultural unbalance that we discussed previously. It fits with our culture's worship of youth and of the new. Anything old is outmoded and to be discarded, and unfortunately that often includes old people. Sadly, it is common to see seniors talked down to or else ignored. As you get older you become increasingly invisible. I am just starting to experience that.

年龄特有的德性The Special Virtues of Age

老年特有的德性，与我试图在本书中重新找回的土星种种品质交织在一起。我想在这里考察其中一些德性，也想公允看待年龄特有的困难，即这幅图景中凶性的一面——事物散架、停止运作时会遇到的困难。老年既有特殊挑战，也有特殊馈赠；我无意淡化任何一面。
The special virtues of old age are woven in with the different qualities of Saturn that I am trying to recover in this book. I want to consider some of those virtues here. I also want to do justice to the particular difficulties of age, the malefic side of the picture, those difficulties you have when things fall apart and quit functioning. Old age has special challenges, and also special gifts, and I don't care to minimize either dimension.
如今要做到这一点格外困难，因为在我们的文化里，这一人生阶段的价值及其特有德性实在没有得到承认。人生需要意义感，其中一项需要，就是感到自己有某些有价值之物可以给予、奉献或为他人去做。我们在许多方面已经剥夺了老年人的这种品质与机会。
This is particularly difficult today because the worth of this period of life, its particular virtues, are really not recognized in our culture. One of the needs for a sense of meaning in life is to have the feeling that you have something worthwhile to give, to offer, to do for others. In a lot of ways we have robbed older people of that quality and of that opportunity.
我正逐渐明白，随着年纪增长，我拥有了一种不同的思考与觉知方式。部分原因是视角：能够从当下抽身，退后一步，从外部加以考察。它也来自活得足够久，因而能感知整体周期——观念、风潮与时尚出现、成长、抵达顶峰，继而消退。年轻时，人太容易被当下的风潮或狂热卷走；年纪渐长后，就更容易退后一步，取得视角与距离，把当下事件放进更大的历史语境中考察。
I am learning that I have a different kind of thinking and awareness as I get older. It is partly a matter of perspective, of being able to stand back from the immediate moment and consider it from the outside. It also comes from having lived long enough to get a sense of overall cycles, of ideas, fads and fashions coming, growing, peaking and then fading. When you are young it is all too easy to be swept up in the fad or mania of the moment. As you get older it is easier to stand back and get some perspective and distance, to consider the moment's events in the larger context of history.
要真正践行这种德性、欣赏年龄的这种品质，你需要容许时间与空间存在。你需要时间停下来、思考、沉思；需要独自与思想相处的时间，也需要时间进行获得这种视角所需的种种心智处理。原书 p.199
In order to really practice this virtue, to appreciate this quality of age, you need to allow time and space. You need time to stop, to think, to contemplate. You need time to be alone with your thoughts, and time to do the sorts of mental processing needed to get this perspective.
电视总开着时，或你拴在手机与电脑上时，就无法进行这种思考；让自己始终忙碌不休时，也做不到。我见过的老年生活环境都不利于这种安静时光：每一刻都不断有人打断、都有事物分散注意，无处不在的电视屏幕永远开着；人还常觉得自己始终受到监视，那环境就像温斯顿·史密斯最可怕的噩梦。
You cannot do this kind of thinking when the TV is always on, or when you are tethered to your phone or computer. You cannot do this kind of thinking when you keep yourself constantly busy. The senior environments that I have seen work against this sort of quiet time, as they provide constant interruptions and distractions at every moment, with the ubiquitous TV screen always on, and there is often the experience that you are always being watched, an environment like Winston Smith's worst nightmare.
背景温斯顿·史密斯
温斯顿·史密斯是乔治·奥威尔《一九八四》的主人公；小说中的电幕持续播放，也持续监视。作者借此形容某些老年环境里永不关闭的屏幕与无处独处的感觉。
缓慢、深刻、富有洞见的思考之所以是一种土星品质，自有其原因。若老年过得合宜，这种思考就是老年的特征；而它需要时间、空间与努力才能绽放。
There is a reason why slow, profound, insightful thinking is a Saturnine quality. It is characteristic of old age if you do it right, and it takes time, space and effort to blossom.
老年是你直视自身必死性的时期。死亡正在临近，或快或慢。这个过程剥去你的层层外壳，使你回到根本，回到生命中重要的事。变老、剥除、回归根本、直抵白骨，都是土星概念。
Old age is a time when you are staring your own mortality in the face. Quickly or slowly, death is approaching. This is a process that strips you down to the basics, to the important things in life. Growing old, stripping down, getting to the basics, getting to the bare bones, are all Saturnian concepts.
我还发现另一种品质很能代表晚年。既然我们同时生活在时间与永恒两个世界，老年就是一个好时期，可以逐渐从对时间性关切的依附中退下，把更多时间用于关注永恒之物。
I am finding another quality to be characteristic of the later years. Given that we live in two worlds at once, temporal and eternal, old age is a good period to increasingly withdraw from attachment to temporal concerns and spend more time focusing on what is eternal.
老年可以提供一种特殊机会：拥有时间，只是安静与思考，为思考本身而思考——不是为了计划或行动而思考，而只是为了理解。它很适合在心智中把各种事物联结起来，看见整体图式。这不会偶然发生：它需要意愿，需要时间与空间，也需要练习与持续的功夫。
Old age can offer the special opportunity for time to just be quiet and think, to think for its own sake - not time to think in order to plan or do, just time to think in order to understand. It is a good time for tying things together mentally, seeing the whole pattern. This does not happen by accident: it takes intention, it takes time and space, and it takes practice and sustained work.
如今随着年岁增长，我花相当多时间祈祷（写作此文时我六十七岁），并发现祈祷有了新的维度。我猜这是衰老的自然部分，因为两个世界之间的帷幕变薄了。我对这里的依附更少，要做的工作也更少，所以能够放松对这个世界那种猛烈的专注，让心智以其他方式敞开。原书 p.200
I spend a good amount of time praying now as I am getting older (I am age 67 as of this writing), and I find that prayer takes on a new dimension. I suspect that this is a natural part of aging, as the veil between the worlds gets thinner. I am less attached here, I have less work to do, so I can relax some of the fierce focus on this world and allow my mind to be open in other ways.
过去几年里，为亡者、为先我们而去的人祈祷，对我变得愈发重要。几乎所有传统文化都有尊崇亡者的做法：思念他们，向他们献供，为他们祈祷。我越来越感觉自己需要为亡者祈祷；这是一种内在需要。传统教导我们：有些祈祷我们能为亡者献上，是他们无法为自己做的；亡者也能为我们祈祷，以我们无法自助的方式帮助我们。
Over the past few years it has become increasingly important to me to be praying for the dead, for those who have gone before. Almost all traditional cultures have practices that honor the dead, think about them, make offerings to them and pray for them. I increasingly get the feeling that I need to be praying for the dead. It is an inner need. Tradition teaches us that there are prayers we can offer for the dead that they cannot do for themselves, and that the dead can pray for us and help us in ways we cannot help ourselves.
现代文化让我们失去了一种极其珍贵的东西：与祖先共同组成一个活生生社群的感觉。请留意，这个主题如何与我们对时间、历史、传统和周期的态度相互联结。所有这些概念都交织在一起。
We have lost something very precious in our modern culture, that sense of a living community with our ancestors. Notice how this subject ties together with our attitude towards time, and history, and tradition, and cycles. All of the concepts intertwine.
衰老还有另一个面向：随着我变老，周围的世界也在变化。我发现自己与当前风潮、样式越来越不同步。这一主题出现在安妮·赖斯Anne Rice的一些吸血鬼故事中。在她的世界里，吸血鬼理论上永生，却可能老到与周围世界彻底脱节，因而产生求死的欲望。尘世的不朽也会令人厌倦。我有时会想，自己会不会有一天不再觉得现代世界是家，觉得再也不属于这里。这并不是伤感或自怜；周围世界正以我不愿融入的方式改变。
Yet another aspect of aging happens because, as I grow old, the world around me is changing also. I find myself getting increasingly out of sync with current fads and styles. This is a theme that shows up in some of the Anne Rice vampire stories. In her world the vampires are theoretically immortal, but can age to the point where they are so out of sync with the world around them that they have the desire to die. Immortality on earth can get old. I sometimes wonder if I will get to a point where I no longer feel at home in the modern world, that I will feel like I don't fit in anymore. I don't mean this in a maudlin or self-pitying sense; the world around me changes in ways that I don't want to fit in with.
我发现自己想思考自身的死亡。我不认为这很病态；我认为，人年纪渐长时这样做完全正常而自然。对我来说，要找到能够轻松自然地谈论我自己死亡的人并不容易。我猜，对于自己的衰老与死亡，我比大多数朋友都更自在。
I find myself wanting to think about my own death. I do not think this is being morbid, I think it is perfectly normal and natural to do this as one gets older. It is difficult for me to find people to easily and naturally talk about my own death. I suspect I am more at ease with my own aging and death than most of my friends.
人的确可能抵达这样一个时刻：已经活得够久了。原书 p.201
There is such a thing as getting to a point where you have lived long enough.
我们的医疗体系以及文化中的很大一部分，都执迷于尽可能延长生命的观念与实践，不顾生活质量与代价，采取激烈手段。如今这似乎开始改变，因为我所属的庞大婴儿潮世代正在变老、接近死亡。我越来越常见到一些运动，旨在提高人们对死亡质量的意识，选择何时以及如何死去。
Our medical system, and much of our culture, is obsessed with the idea and practice of extending life as much as possible, taking drastic measures with no regard to quality of life or to cost. That seems to be starting to change now, with my very large Baby Boomer generation getting old and approaching death. I am increasingly seeing movements to raise awareness about quality of death, choosing when and how to die.
老年不必成为敌人。它自有独特的宝藏等待开启，而我才刚开始发现它们。
Old age does not need to be an enemy. It has its own special treasures to be unlocked, and I am just starting to discover them.

苦难Suffering

苦难是人生自然且可以预期的一部分。期待一生没有苦难，完全不切实际。既然生活中存在苦难，我们就必须在占星学中承认苦难。不能把它轻描淡写或加以淡化；应当接受并应对它。原书 p.202
Suffering is a natural and expected part of life. It is completely unrealistic to expect to have a life without suffering. If there is suffering in our lives, then we must acknowledge suffering in our astrology. It is not to be glossed over, or minimized; it is to be accepted and dealt with.
充分证据表明，应对一定程度的苦难，是成长与成熟的必要部分。受到过度保护、远离苦难的人，会缺少某种成熟与力量，缺少逆境真正到来时应对它的能力。而逆境随时都可能、也必然会到来。
There is good evidence that dealing with a certain amount of suffering is a necessary part of growing up and maturing. People who are overly shielded from suffering lack a certain maturity and strength, an ability to deal with adversity when it does come. And, adversity can and will come at any time.
疼痛也是如此，这里我说的是身体疼痛。疼痛与苦难并不完全相同，但两者确实相关。一定程度的疼痛——有时是剧烈疼痛——也是人类生活常见的一部分。只有在富裕而备受庇护的现代世界里，我们才可能期待一切疼痛都得到治疗或被彻底驱除。
It is the same with pain, and here I am talking about physical pain. Pain is not quite the same thing as suffering, but the two are definitely related. A certain amount of pain, sometimes severe pain, is also a common part of human life. It is only in our affluent and sheltered modern world that we could expect all pain to be treated or banished.
如果苦难得到妥善应对，它可以在内在成长方面产生正面结果。我认为，体验苦难是人生目的的一部分，这话确实成立；苦难不是偶然或不幸的副作用。苦难之中有某种东西，与我们此生的目的贴得非常近。
Suffering can have a positive outcome in terms of inner growth if it is dealt with well. I think it is true that part of the purpose of a human life is to experience suffering, that it is not an incidental or unfortunate side-effect. There is something about suffering that cuts very close to the purpose of our life here.
态度在这里会造成很大差别。若要学习如何善处苦难，很值得研读伟大的斯多葛哲学家马可·奥勒留Marcus Aurelius、爱比克泰德Epictetus与塞涅卡Seneca。
Attitude makes a large difference here, and it is worth studying the great Stoic philosophers, Marcus Aurelius, Epictetus and Seneca, for an education on dealing well with suffering.
人类的许多苦难属于第二层苦难second level suffering，也就是「对于苦难的苦难」：我们对苦难的反应使它加剧。若把这两个层次分开，我们对第一层苦难的控制极其有限，但对于如何回应苦难、自己的回应会减轻还是加重它，却有很大的选择余地。
Much human suffering is second level suffering, or suffering about suffering, where our reaction to suffering increases it. Thinking of these two levels, we have greatly limited control over suffering, but we have a great deal of choice as to how we respond to suffering, and if our response minimizes it or adds to it.
术语“善处苦难”与第二层苦难
Suffering well 在全书定译「善处苦难」。它不把受苦本身视为善，而是区分不可控的第一层苦难与回应所增加的第二层苦难；「善处」就是尽量不让恐惧、抗拒、罪疚或自责再叠加一层。
苦难是任何改变与成长过程中的自然部分。例如，可以想想苦难的不同阶段如何纳入哀悼过程。善处并走过那段苦难，能让哀悼变得有力而具有正面意义；不肯面对它，则会制造进一步的阻滞与问题。原书 p.203
Suffering is a natural part of any process of change and growth. For example, considering how differing phases of suffering fit into the grief process. Going through that suffering well can make grieving powerful and positive; failing to face it can create further blocks and problems.

救赎性苦难Redemptive Suffering

这里我还想触及苦难的另一个维度：苦难可以具有救赎性redemptive——为了帮助他人，自愿把苦难承担到自己身上。救赎性的自愿受苦这一概念在基督宗教中尤其强烈，又以罗马天主教为最，是传统深刻而持久的一部分。既然数千年来有如此多伟大人物拥抱救赎性苦难，我会假定这种信念有充分理由，也假定它不只是精神错乱或病态。
There is a another dimension of suffering I want to touch on here. This is the idea that suffering can be redemptive, a voluntary suffering taken on oneself in order to help others. This concept of redemptive voluntary suffering is particularly strong in the Christian religion, especially Roman Catholic, where it is a deep and enduring part of the tradition. When so very many great people have embraced redemptive suffering for thousands of years I assume that there is a good reason for that belief, and I assume it is not just a derangement or a pathology.
这种我们能以受苦彼此帮助的观念，又与我们需要彼此代祷的观念相关。其他人——无论生者还是亡者——能以我无法为自己祈祷的方式替我祈祷。在天主教体系中，这就是诸圣相通communion of Saints：我们所有穿行于时间中的人、所有曾经活过或将要生活的人共同组成社群；我们能够共同受苦、彼此帮助、彼此支持，并且作为人类共同体，有义务彼此代祷、彼此扶助。
This idea that we can suffer to assist each other is in turn related to the idea that we need to pray for each other. There are ways that other people, living or dead, can pray for me that I cannot pray for myself. In the Catholic system this is the communion of Saints, the community of all of us going through time, all who have ever lived or will live, and that we can suffer together, help each other, support each other, and that we have an obligation to pray for each other and to help each other as a human community.
这触及集体苦难的奥秘：我们所有人的苦难彼此相连。我们从不独自受苦，总是与他人一同受苦、为他人受苦；他人的苦难在深层影响我们所有人。唯有我们过的并非彼此分离的个体生活，唯有我们的生命必须放在一个更大整体的语境中才有道理、有意义，这种信念与实践才说得通。我们所有人的生命都彼此相连；没有谁仅仅为自己而生或为自己而死。
This touches on the mystery of collective suffering, the idea that all of our suffering is linked with each other. We never suffer alone, we always suffer with and for others, and other people's suffering affects all of us at a deep level. Such a belief and practice only makes sense if we do not live separate individual lives, but that our lives make sense and have meaning only in context of a larger whole. All of our lives are connected; none of us lives or dies to ourself alone.
天主教祈祷实践有一个面向：人可以把自己的苦难奉献出来，作为给他人的礼物或医治。在这样的脉络里，人甚至可能抱持欢迎苦难或自愿承担它的态度。这种态度把苦难本身视为正面而美好的事物。如我所说，我太常在一些最伟大的圣人生命中看到这一点，无法把它斥为纯粹偏差或病态。
There is a dimension of Catholic prayer practice where a person can offer up their own suffering as a gift or a healing for others. In such a setting there can be an attitude even of welcoming suffering or voluntarily taking it on. This attitude views suffering itself as something positive and beautiful. As I said, I have seen that far too often in the lives of some of the greatest of saints for me to dismiss that as a mere aberration or a pathology.
我确实从自身经验知道，把自己的苦难奉献出来帮助他人，会减轻苦难与疼痛。这样会使它更容易承受，并赋予它意义感与目的感。在这种脉络里，苦难不再是需要躲避或逃离之物。原书 p.204
I do know from my own experience that offering up my own suffering to help others does lighten the suffering and pain. It makes it easier to bear, and gives it a sense of meaning and purpose. Suffering in such a setting is no longer something to avoid or run from.
我们仍然应该尽己所能减轻苦难。许多最伟大的圣人正是这样做的，例如加尔各答的德肋撒修女Mother Theresa of Calcutta，以及美国的多萝西·戴Dorothy Day；她们通常不让任何人的注意力落在自己承受的苦难上。
We should still do all we can to alleviate suffering. Many of the greatest saints like Mother Theresa of Calcutta and Dorothy Day here in America have done just that, usually without drawing any attention to the suffering they undergo themselves.
正如先前谈到的，这揭示了另一个事实：最糟的苦难实际上是第二层的，也就是对于苦难的苦难。即便只处理身体疼痛，你也会发现，绷紧身体对抗、与它搏斗时，疼痛会糟得多；放松下来，向疼痛之中呼吸时，它更容易承受。拥抱疼痛会使它更容易承受。
As we touched on earlier, this uncovers another truth, that the worst of suffering is actually second level, or suffering about suffering. Even dealing with strictly physical pain, you can learn that pain is far worse when you tense up against it and fight it, and that it is easier to bear when relax and breathe into it. Embracing pain makes it easier to bear.
拥抱苦难，以及为他人受苦，都是放松下来、呼吸进入慈悲奥秘的方式；正是这奥秘把我们所有人联结在一起。
Embracing suffering, and suffering for others, are ways of relaxing and breathing into a mystery of compassion that ties us all together.

爱德Caritas

最后，我想谈谈由爱德Caritas而受苦。拉丁词 Caritas 的含义深度与丰富程度，远超任何英语对应词。它包含一种深厚的无私，是英语 love 一词所缺少的；也比今天已经贬值的英语 Charity 深邃得多，后者已经退化得近似只指给穷人的免费施舍。
Finally, I want to touch here on suffering from Caritas. The Latin word Caritas has a depth and richness of meaning far greater than any English equivalent. It connotes a depth of selflessness missing in our word love, and it is worlds deeper than the now cheapened English word Charity which has degenerated to mean something like free handouts for the poor.
爱德不是一种情绪，更像一股活生生的力量。用宗教语言说，可以把它理解为神圣者创造并维系宇宙的慈悲仁爱。爱德的力量，是创造诸世界并把它们联结在一起的力量。
Caritas is not an emotion but is more like a living force. In religious terms you can think of it as the merciful loving-kindness of the Divine that creates and sustains the universe. The power of Caritas is the power that creates worlds and that binds them together.
术语Caritas 译“爱德”
这是基督宗教传统术语，既不同于一般情感意义的「爱」，也不同于现代语境中狭义的慈善施舍。作者把它理解为创造、维系并联结世界的神圣仁爱力量，所以保留拉丁词并采用通行译名「爱德」。
当我们以爱德受苦时，我们向他人的苦难敞开心灵。我们怜悯一个人，正如上帝怜悯所有人；我们把自己的苦难与关怀，同上帝的苦难与关怀联结起来。当我们通过爱德受苦时，上帝通过我们施予怜悯。基督宗教传统中的伟大神秘家一致说，这样的怜悯以及我们自愿承担的苦难，具有促成善的巨大力量。原书 p.205
When we suffer with Caritas, we open our heart to the suffering of others. We have compassion for a person in the same way that God has compassion for all people, and we join our suffering and our caring to God's. When we suffer through Caritas God has compassion through us. The great mystics of the Christian tradition are unanimous in saying that such compassion and voluntary suffering on our part has a great power for good.
请花些时间，重新思考你对苦难的态度。
Consider spending time rethinking your attitude towards suffering.

死亡Death

为了说明这一章的主旨，我想讲述亲身经历的两次家人死亡。其中一次是非常丑陋而艰难的死亡；将近三十年后的第二次，则是非常美丽、蒙受祝福的死亡。这是我一生最具塑造力的两件事，对我如何看待身为人、身为必死者，以及如何看待死亡与临终，都产生了深远影响。原书 p.206
In order to make my point in this chapter I want to tell the story of two deaths that I have experienced personally in my family. One of them was a very ugly and hard death. The second one, nearly thirty years later, was a very beautiful and blessed death. These were two of the most formative events in my entire life, and they went far to shaping my attitude towards being human and mortal and towards death and dying.
两人都死于癌症。
Both were deaths from cancer.
第一次，一位年轻亲属被诊断出黑色素瘤，一种致命的皮肤癌。经过治疗后，疾病一度缓解，继而复发。复发后大约一年他便去世了；在那段时期，大家都知道他存活的机会非常渺茫。
In the first, a young relative of mine was diagnosed with melanoma, a deadly form of skin cancer. It was treated and went into remission, and then returned. After the return it took about a year before he died, and during that period it was known that his chances of surviving were very low.
是什么使这次死亡变得丑陋？他否认死亡，与它搏斗到最痛苦的尽头——据我所知，一直持续到最后一天。临终几日，他由父母在家照料，也死在家中。我对他最后的记忆，是去世前几周在家里的客厅；那时他已经几乎只剩一副骨架。我记得他说过的唯一一句话是：「让我撑下去。让我撑下去。」
What made this death ugly? He denied and fought that death to the very bitter end - to the last day, as far as I know. In the final days he was cared for at home by his parents, and he died at home. My last memory of him, a few weeks before he died, was there in the family living room, with him little more than a skeleton at this point. And the only words I remember him saying were, Keep me going. Keep me going.
这给整个家庭——他的父母和所有亲属——造成的消耗极其残酷。死亡悬在那间屋子里，鲜明得仿佛可以闻到；却没有人能够说出那个词，因为他还没准备好听见。
The toll on the family, on his parents and all of the relatives, was absolutely brutal. Death was there hanging in the room, so vivid you could smell it - and no-one could say the word, because he wasn't ready to hear it.
他终于去世后，父母不得不在死后的最初几周安排安葬与追思礼。此前没有作过任何计划。他们处理这一切时非常坚强而宽厚，但情感代价想必极其惨烈。
When he finally did die, his parents needed to make the arrangements for his burial, and his memorial service, during those first few weeks after his death. No plans had been made. They were very strong and gracious in how they handled it, but the emotional cost must have been brutal.
让我撑下去。让我撑下去。原书 p.207
Keep me going. Keep me going.
第二次死亡是我的妻子辛迪；她被诊断出第四期乳腺癌。她经历了几年通常的癌症治疗，即使人衰弱的化疗与放疗。后来到了一个时刻，几乎所有人同时看清治疗不起作用——辛迪、我、她的医生和我的女儿都是如此。我们差不多同时准备好向彼此说出消息：「这没有用。她要死了。」
The second death was my wife Cindy, who was diagnosed with stage 4 breast cancer. She went through a couple of years of the usual cancer treatment, the debilitating chemotherapy and radiation. There came a point where it became obvious to pretty much everyone at once that the treatment was not working - to Cindy, to me, to her doctor, to my daughter. We all were ready at about the same time to break the news to each other and to say, this isn't working. She's dying.
辛迪和我应对这件事的方式，只是接受消息。我们在客厅里安排了居家临终关怀；她生命最后三个月里，我一直照顾她。在那段时期，朋友与亲属接连不断来到家里，陪伴我们。她去世前大约一个月，我们趁她仍在世时为她举行了一场公开追思礼，让她能够亲身经历。那场仪式是我们共同生活中的巅峰之日，也是我一生的巅峰之日之一。仪式包括致敬讲话、一些肚皮舞与许多无伴奏合唱；结束时，我们一首接一首地唱，教堂里的每个人——足有数百人，教堂坐得满满的——都有机会走到辛迪面前道别。
Cindy and I dealt with that by just accepting the news. We set up home hospice in our living room, and I took care of Cindy during those last three months of her life before she died. During that period we had a steady stream of visitors to our house, of friends and relatives coming to spend time with us. About a month before she died we did a public memorial service for her, while she was still alive, so that she could experience it. That service was a peak day in our lives together, one of the peak days of my entire life. After the service, which included tribute speeches, some belly dancing and a lot of a cappella singing, we had a period at the end where we sang song after song while everyone in the church - hundreds of people, the church was full - got a chance to come up to Cindy and say goodbye.
爱将辛迪完全围绕，而她知道。
Cindy was completely surrounded by love and she knew it.
那场仪式几天后，她的身体开始迅速衰退，几周后在我们的客厅去世。她生命的最后三个月，对我是一段登临峰顶的经验。单凭最后这三个月，我们此前共同度过的所有岁月就都值得。
A few days after that service she started rapidly declining, and died in our living room a few weeks later. That last three months of her life was a mountain-top experience for me. It was worth all the rest of our years together just for those last three months.
若回想家人和朋友圈中我见过的其他死亡，很遗憾，对死亡的第一种态度似乎在我们的文化中更为普遍。人受到强迫，要勇敢地战斗下去，要采取一切可能手段、付出一切可能代价——然后或许直到死前一两天，才终于承认死亡不可避免。这样几乎不留任何时间，让人道别、和解、安排身后事务等等。
If I consider the deaths I have seen others experience in my family and community of friends, unfortunately it seems that the first attitude towards death is the more prevalent in our culture. There is the compulsion to fight bravely on, to take every possible measure and every possible expense - and maybe, get around to admitting that death is inevitable maybe a day or two before it occurs. That leaves next to no time for people to say goodbye, make their peace, make their plans and so on.
想想你曾多少次听人说，某某输掉了与癌症或其他疾病的英勇战斗。死亡是敌人，必须咬紧牙关战斗到最后一刻。原书 p.208
Think of the number of times you have seen people say that so and so lost their brave battle against cancer, or whatever disease. Death is the enemy to be fought tooth and nail to the last instant.
让我撑下去。
Keep me going.
幸运的是，随着我所属的庞大婴儿潮世代步入人生最后几年，我看到人们对死亡的态度发生了显著转变，也看到更多资源投入使死亡本身成为正面经验。人们正在认识到，善终可以是一份美妙、由上帝赐予的祝福，是一生最终绽放的冠冕之花。
Fortunately, as my very large baby boom generation is moving into our final years, I am seeing a significant shift in attitude towards death, and I am seeing more resources dedicated to making death itself positive. People are realizing that a good death can be a wonderful and God-given blessing, a crowning blossom on a life.
提示“善终”不是战败
作者以两次家庭经验对照：否认死亡会压缩道别、和解与安排的时间；承认必死性则可能让临终仍有关系、仪式与爱的空间。「善终」不是不治疗，也不是把死亡美化，而是死亡不可避免时，不再把它只当作一场必须赢下的战争。
死亡就是土星。与土星抗争或否认土星毫无益处，只会造成更多、更深的苦难。接受土星并与之合作，则能产生祝福——是的，那是严酷的祝福，但仍然深刻而富有意义。
Death is Saturn. Fighting or denying Saturn does no good, and just causes more and deeper suffering. Accepting and working with Saturn can produce blessings - hard blessings, yes, but deep and meaningful blessings nonetheless.

敬畏上帝Fear of God

灵性成熟包含这样的认识：你不是宇宙的中心，世界并不围绕你转动；你受一种更伟大的律法与力量管辖。你永远站在活生生的永恒律法面前。原书 p.209
Spiritual maturity involves realizing that you are not the center of the universe, that the world doesn't revolve around you, and that you are under the jurisdiction of a greater law and a greater power. You are always standing in the presence of the living Eternal Law.
我认为，这就是「敬畏上帝是智慧的开端」这句话中的真理。
I think that is the truth to the Fear of God being the beginning of wisdom.
我明白这句话已经过时了。我正试着温和地接近一件非常重要、却已大体消失在现代文化视野之外的事。
I realize this phrase is out of style. I am attempting to gently approach something very important here that our modern day culture has largely lost sight of.
你的选择重要，你的思想重要，你的行动重要，而且都会产生后果。这不表示你的思想控制现实，而是说你的思想与行动存在于一个更大的语境中，并在其中产生后果。这包括认识到，宇宙内在与外在结构里确实嵌有永恒的道德律，而同样的律法也嵌在人心中央。这种认识必须深入肺腑，成为经验与情感，才能真正有效、真正改变人生。
Your choices matter, your thoughts matter, your actions matter and have consequences. This does not mean that your thoughts control reality. It does mean that your thoughts and actions exist in a larger context, and have consequences in that larger context. This includes realizing that there really are eternal moral laws wired into the structure of the universe, inner and outer, and those same laws are wired into the center of the human heart. This realization needs to be gut level, experiential and emotional to really be effective and life-changing.
宇宙的运行方式之中，确实有一条永恒的正义之律。如果你的行为不正义，它迟早会弄巧成拙，后果也会追上你。这是柏拉图《理想国》的主题，也是世界各地传统中其他伟大伦理著作的主题。
There really is an eternal law of justice that is part of how the universe works. If you act in unjust ways, sooner or later they are self-defeating and will catch up to you. This is the main theme of Plato's Republic, and of other great ethical works from traditions all over the world.
还有另一种认识更难诉诸语言。它部分是观念，部分是感受，部分是超越两者的深刻经验。
There is another realization harder to put into words. It is part idea, part feeling, part deep experience beyond either of those.
在这种经验发生以前，我们不知为何会认为，自己真正最内在的思想与情感除了自己以外，对所有人、所有存在都隐藏着。我们内心深处觉得思想是私密的，外在于自己的一切都绝不可能知道我们心底隐秘处的所思所感。与此相伴的感觉是：只要能把事情藏好，不知怎的就可以逃脱后果。儿童的行为仿佛就带着这种感觉，在周围成人看来可能颇为有趣。我们一生中的许多时候，都像那些儿童一样。
Prior to this experience we somehow think that our real innermost thoughts and emotions are hidden from everyone and everything but ourselves. Deep inside of us is the feeling that our thoughts are private, and that no-one outside of ourselves could possibly know what we think and feel in the privacy of our hearts. Along with this is the feeling that we can somehow get away with doing things provided we can keep them hidden. Children act like they have that sense, and it can be amusing to the adults around them. We are all like those children for much of our lives.
如果我们非常、非常幸运，就会迎来那个美丽而令人敬畏的时刻，认识到一些重要真理。原书 p.210
If we are very, very fortunate, there comes that beautiful and awesome moment when we realize some important truths.
我们认识到，自己始终被看见。
We realize that we are always seen.
我们认识到，自己始终被知晓。
We realize that we are always known.
我们认识到，内在有「那一位」，知晓我们每一个动作、每一个念头。
We realize that there is That within us which knows our every move and every thought.
我们认识到，一生的每一刻，自己的每一句话、每一个行为都必须承担责任。
We realize that every moment of our lives we are held accountable for our every word and deed.
世界各地的灵性传统都教导说，终有一个时刻，我们必须交代自己的一生。后来有一刻，我们会认识到，必须作出这番交代的时刻——永远就是现在。这就是我所说的敬畏上帝。《圣经》说得对：敬畏上帝是智慧的开端。它也是智慧的终点、智慧的源头，并且是通往爱上帝的门。
Spiritual traditions all over the world teach that that there will come a time when we must give an account of our lives. There comes a moment we realize that the time when we must give that account - is always right now. That is what I mean by the Fear of God. And the Bible is right, the fear of God is the beginning of wisdom. It is also the end of wisdom, and the source of wisdom, and the doorway to the love of God.
出处“敬畏上帝是智慧的开端”
这一表述见《箴言》9:10 与《诗篇》111:10 等处。这里的「敬畏」不只是害怕惩罚，而是人在永恒律法面前意识到自己被看见、被知晓，并须为每一刻负责。
我是时间中的存在，永远站在永恒面前。这是一种非常土星式的经验。我认为，这就是为什么古代文献常把土星与智慧联系起来。
I am a being in time, always standing in the presence of eternity. This is a very Saturnian sort of experience. This, I think, is why Saturn is often related to wisdom in the old texts.
土星连接时间与永恒两个世界；敬畏上帝这种非常土星式的经验，使我们更接近一切的源头。
Saturn bridges the worlds of time and eternity, and the very Saturnian experience of the Fear of God brings us much closer to the source of all.

谦卑Humility

谦卑是一种特别属于土星的德性，也是一种在现代文化中已经大体失落、遭到贬低的德性。对现代世界来说，骄傲与自尊受到远为强烈的强调，也远为重要。谦卑与现代文化大相径庭，因为后者尽是自我伸张与自我显现。原书 p.211
Humility is a particularly Saturnian virtue, and one that we have largely lost and devalued in our modern culture. For the modern world, pride and self-esteem are far more emphasized and far more important. Humility runs very much against the grain of our modern culture which is all about self-assertion and self-visibility.
现代世界聚焦于实现人的潜能、把欲望与梦想付诸行动，于是崇拜内在神性很容易悄然滑向自我崇拜。若没有更高力量在我们之上，我们便把个体之人的价值擢升到至高。
With our modern world focused on the fulfillment of our human potential, and of acting out our desires and dreams, worshipping the inner divinity slides very easily over into self-worship. With no higher power above us we exalt individual human worth.
阿维拉的德肋撒圣女Saint Theresa of Avila这样的人，正是在这里形成了极鲜明的对照。如果读过她的任何作品，就会看到一种完全彻底的自我隐退，听起来仿佛她不给自己任何价值；然而，这位女性却拥有惊人的力量、意志力与坚忍。
This is where people like Saint Theresa of Avila provide such a very marked contrast. If you have read any of her writing, there is a complete and total self-effacement that sounds like she is giving no value to herself; and yet the woman had an astounding strength, will-power and perseverance.
这指出了谦卑如何纳入我们一直在建立的其余意义结构。用德肋撒自己的话说，她知道凭自己什么也做不了，但与上帝同在便凡事都能。
This points to where humility fits in with the rest of the structure of meaning we have been building. In her own terms, Theresa knew that by herself she could do nothing, but with God she could do all things.
不要让任何事扰乱你，
不要让任何事惊吓你；
一切都会过去，
上帝永不改变；
耐心坚忍
成就一切；
拥有上帝的人
一无所缺；
唯有上帝已足够。
——阿维拉的德肋撒
Let nothing disturb thee, Nothing affright thee All things are passing; God never changeth; Patient endurance Attaineth to all things; Who God possesseth In nothing is wanting; God alone sufficeth. - Theresa of Avila
德肋撒认识到，我们拥有来自宇宙结构与实在本身的内在支持。它既在我们之内，也在我们之外；既位于我们的最核心处，又远远超越每一个个体，所以绝不会被误认成个人自我。
Theresa realized that we have an internal support from the very structure and reality of the universe itself. It is within us and without us. It is at our very core, but also far, far beyond each of us individually, so that there is no risk of mistaking it for our personal selves.
当我们使自己与宇宙秩序对齐，
就不再需要看重
自己个体的价值。原书 p.212
When we align ourselves with the order of the universe, there is no longer the need to place importance on our own individual worth.
我们自身孤立的个体价值，并不重要，也不会在死后存续。
Our own individual isolated worth is not what is important, and is not what survives death.
正是在我们觉知永恒世界、站在永恒律法面前时，谦卑、慈悲与正义这样的价值才变得至关重要。它们指向永恒之物、真实之物，也就是在我们的时间性经验之后仍会存续之物。
It is when we stand in awareness of the eternal world, in the presence of eternal law, that values like Humility and Mercy and Justice become all important. They point to what is eternal, what is real and survives our temporal experience.
谦卑是那种美妙而使人解放的认识：作为个体的我并没有那么重要。然而，作为与源头相连的个体，我又具有永恒而无限的价值。若我能够不再想着自己和自己的重要性，专注于眼前交付给我的任务，尽己所能工作，却不忧虑自己或工作的结果，这种认识就最有效。重要的不是我，而是有待完成的工作、有待履行的服务。
Humility is that wonderful and liberating realization that I as an individual just am not that important. And yet, I as an individual as connected connected to the source have an eternal worth and infinite worth. This realization is most effective when I am able to stop thinking about myself and my own importance and concentrate on my given task at hand, working to the best of my ability without worrying about myself or the outcome of my work. What is important is not me, but the work to be done, the service to be performed.
从某些方面说，谦卑绝对是我最喜爱的德性，因为人不可能宣称自己拥有它。就在我为自己宣称谦卑的行为中，我已经不再谦卑。它是一种必须对自身隐形的德性。
In some ways humility is my absolute favorite of the virtues, in that it is impossible to claim it for oneself. In the very act of claiming humility for myself I cease to be humble. It is a virtue that must be invisible to itself.
土星式谦卑与隐形、无私服务相关，是太阳式自我伸张的正相反面。它关乎在可见范围之外、公众视线之外完成的工作，不把功劳据为己有。它隐退自我，也抹去自我。谦卑处于低位，却有一种骄傲永远不可能拥有、而现代世界已大体失去的优势。
Saturnian humility relates to invisibility and to selfless service. It is the direct opposite of the self-assertion of the Sun. It deals with work done outside of visibility, outside of the public eye, with no credit taken. It is self-effacing and self-erasing. Being lowly, humility has an advantage that pride can never have, an advantage we have largely lost in our modern world.
骄傲只能俯视；谦卑能够仰望。
Pride can only look down; humility can look up.

结语Final Thoughts

撰写本书初稿期间，我接受手术，切除了一个癌性肿瘤。如今我仍在处理术后影响，不知道结果如何。癌症诊断为本书主题的写作增添了个人而迫切的意味；我努力分享其中一些迫切感，尤其是在第三部分的文章中。原书 p.213
While working on the first draft of this book I had surgery to remove a cancerous tumor. I am still dealing with the aftermath of that now, and I do not know how it will turn out. Being diagnosed with cancer gives the work on the themes of this book a personal and urgent note, and I have tried to share some of that urgency, especially in the essays in part three.
我正克制一种冲动，不想在书末用一个整齐漂亮的蝴蝶结，把所有主题都捆扎起来。与占星学中的其他一切一样，土星的含义复杂而多维；这一切带着某种辉煌、鲜活的凌乱。这里我只想概括写作本书的主要目的。
I am resisting the urge to take all the themes of this book and tie them up in a nice neat bow here at the end. Like everything else in astrology, the meanings of Saturn are complex and multi-dimensional, and there is a sort of glorious living sloppiness to it all. Here I want to sum my main purposes for writing this book.
