在学习一般占星学、尤其是理解土星时，我想强调，以传统材料为基础是何等重要。它没有为现代心智整理成齐整体系，但其中蕴藏着丰富的细节与意义。我试着抽出一些主要主题，也确信还有更多主题等待发现。第一部分的历史纵览，是进一步探索的良好起点。
In learning astrology in general, and understanding Saturn in particular, I want to emphasize how very important it is to build on the traditional material. It is not neatly systematized for our modern minds, but there is a wealth of detail and meaning there. I tried to pull out some of the main themes, and I am sure there are more to be found. The historical survey in Part One is a good place to start explore further.
要在传统占星学的语境中理解土星，我们就必须觉察现代世界观的假设与局限。我试图把注意力引向我们容易具有的一些盲点；希望这能使你自己的态度少一点确信，多一点试探与开放。
To understand Saturn in the context of traditional astrology we need to be aware of the assumptions and limitations of our modern world view. I have tried to focus attention on some of the blind spots we tend to have, and hopefully this may make your own attitude a bit less sure, a bit more tentative and open-minded.
即便现代人对宇宙的理解发生了改变，我仍在本书中论证：宇宙有一套永恒的律法与图式，既存在于我们心智之内，也存在于其外。更重要的是，我还试图论证，宇宙的结构中嵌有永恒的道德律。这令人敬畏，也令人惊叹；希望你能以新的方式瞥见它。当我们仰望诸天，看到的并不是空无的浩瀚空间，而是永恒的秩序、图式与节律。它是活的，而我们是其中一部分。
Even with the change in our understanding of the cosmos in modern times, I make a case in this book for there being an eternal law and pattern to the Universe that is both within and outside our minds. More importantly I also tried to make the case that the Universe has a hard-wired eternal moral law. There is an awe and a wonder to that, and hopefully you can catch sight of it in a new way. When we look into the Heavens we do not see empty expanses of space; we see eternal order, pattern and rhythm. It is alive, and we are part of it.
真正把土星视为时间与永恒两个世界之间的边界时，这颗行星才最讲得通；而我们也身处同样的边界。只有放进这一语境，土星含义中的深度与维度才会显露。原书 p.214
Saturn really makes most sense when we consider the planet as being at the border between two worlds, temporal and eternal, as are we. There are depths and dimensions to Saturn's meaning that are revealed only in that context.
最后，我还试图把土星的一些艰难主题带到光下，重新考察苦难、恶、疼痛、老年与死亡——在过分舒适的现代世界里，我们宁愿移开视线的人生面向。我们需要人生这些严酷面向，才能成为完整的人；它们不会只因我们希望它们消失就离去。
And finally, I tried to bring to light and re-examine some of the difficult Saturn themes - of suffering, of evil, of pain, old age and death, aspects of life we'd just as soon look away from in our all-too-comfortable modern world. We need those hard aspects of life to be fully human, and they're not going away just because we'd like them to.
我想以土星式的祝福结束这本书。
I'd like to close this book by offering you Saturnian blessings.
愿你拥抱自己的苦难。愿你拥抱自己的衰老。时刻到来时，愿你拥抱并迎接自己的死亡。那里有无价的馈赠，无法通过任何其他方式获得。
May you embrace your suffering. May you embrace your aging. When the time comes, may you embrace and welcome your own death. There are gifts there beyond price that can be had in no other way.
愿对神圣者、太一与至善、一切之源的鲜活觉知，在你此岸与彼岸的旅程中，与你同在，也在你之内。
May a living awareness of the Divine, the One and the Good, the Source of All, be with you and in you in your journey, here and beyond.
提示土星式祝福
结尾把全书的两条主线合在一起：土星既指向苦难、衰老与死亡，也守在时间与永恒的边界。祝福不是要求读者喜欢痛苦，而是愿人不再以否认增加「第二层苦难」，并在有限生命中保持对永恒秩序的觉知。
APPENDIX

附录

Appendix

“Science”一词含义的变迁Science: the Changed Meaning of the Word

按照我们今天对「科学」一词的理解，占星学不是一门科学。原书 p.215
Astrology is not a science as we currently understand the word science.
要把占星学视为 science，我们就需要重新思考这个词是什么意思，以及它对世界、对何为真实作出了怎样的说明。大多数人没有意识到，过去两百年里，science 一词的含义发生了剧烈改变。这正是我想在这里考察的事。为此，我们要回到语言历史中，找回「Science」一词在上个世纪失落的部分含义。
For astrology to be viewed as a science we need to rethink what we mean by the word, and what that says about the world, and about what is real. Most people don’t realize that the meaning of the word science has changed drastically over the past two hundred years. That is what I want to look at here. This involves going back into the history of our language, and recovering some of the meaning of the term Science that has been lost in the last century.
术语本章保留 science
本章讨论的是英语 science 的词义史，不等同于中文「科学」一词自身的历史。现代语境译「科学」；旧义依上下文译「学问」「知识体系」或保留 science，以免读者误以为各段都在使用今天的经验科学概念。
我想举一个较早文本中的例子，其中 science 的用法显然不同于我们今天的用法。
I want to give an example here of an older text where the use of science is obviously different from how we currently use the term.
下面的引文出自英国柏拉图主义者托马斯·泰勒Thomas Taylor；他大约写作于三百年前。泰勒所指的是柏拉图对话录《巴门尼德篇》的一段，其中的逻辑进程用一系列否定，来证明绝对存在或上帝不可能是什么——上帝既不存在，也并非不存在。整套逻辑完全抽象，不涉及任何经验数据。
The following quote is from the British Platonist Thomas Taylor, who was writing around 300 years ago. He is referring to a passage in the dialog Parmenides by Plato where the train of logic is using a series of negations to prove what an absolute being or God could not be – god neither exists nor does not exist. The entire train of logic is abstract and makes no reference to any empirical data.
在晚期柏拉图主义的形而上学中，这一逻辑进程被视为指向太一与至善的观念；太一与至善是万物的最高本原，依定义超越我们对它所能构想或言说的一切，甚至超越「它具有存在」这种说法。下面是泰勒的引文，粗体强调为我所加。
In the late Platonist metaphysics this train of logic is taken as pointing to a conception of the One and the Good, the highest principle of things, that by definition is beyond everything we can conceive or say about it, including saying that it has being. The quote from Taylor follows here, the emphasis in bold is mine.
这里必须留意，关于万物最高本原的这一结论——即它完全不可言说、不可构想——乃是一个最具学问性的系列所产生的结果……因为，最始终如一地区分柏拉图哲学与其他哲学的特征正在于：它的每一个部分都烙有学问的性质。原书 p.216
And here it must be observed that this conclusion respecting the highest principle of things, that he is perfectly ineffable and inconceivable, is the result of a most scientific series… For that which so consistently distinguishes the philosophy of Plato from others is this, that every part of it is stamped with the character of science.
泰勒是第一个把柏拉图全部对话录译成英语的人；此外，他还翻译了晚期柏拉图主义哲学家普罗克洛的一些著作，并认为那些著作按此词义属于 science。
Along with being the first person to translate all of the Platonic dialogs into English, Taylor also translated some works by the late Platonic philosopher Proclus that Taylor considered to be science in this sense of the term.
普罗克洛Proclus的《神学原理》仿照欧几里得《几何原本》写成，他另一本对欧几里得作神学与哲学评注的著作也是如此。全书被安排成一种逻辑而学问性的论证，从逻辑公理出发，在第一本原之上建立系统的概念结构。哲学与神学的结构，就这样被建成一套逻辑而有系统的知识体系。
The Elements of Theology by Proclus is modeled on Euclid’s elements, as is another book by Proclus that is a theological and philosophical commentary on Euclid. It is laid out as a logical and scientific argument, starting with logical axioms and building a systematic conceptual structure built on the first principles. The structure of philosophy and theology is built up as a logical and scientific structure.
普罗克洛卷帙浩繁的《柏拉图神学》铺陈存在巨链的各个层级，并描述每一层的诸神；它同样以紧密推理的论证展开，普罗克洛视之为 science——但在这部著作中，他讨论的包括诸神的各种位阶。对普罗克洛而言，谈论诸神是一种学问。
The massive work by Proclus on The Theology of Plato, laying out the levels of the great chain of being with descriptions of the gods at each level, is similarly laid out as a closely reasoned argument that Proclus viewed as scientific – but in that work Proclus is including discussing various hierarchies of the gods. For Proclus, speaking of the Gods is a form of science.
显然，普罗克洛和托马斯·泰勒对 science 的理解都与今义大不相同；今义只指经验观察与实验。如果泰勒确曾如此使用 science，那么这一含义就必然属于英语语言史的一部分。
Clearly, both Proclus and Thomas Taylor had a very different conception of science than the current meaning referring only to empirical observation and experiment. If Taylor used science in that way then that meaning must be part of the history of the English language.
幸运的是，我们有办法找回这段历史的一部分。词典是很好的起点：我们可以比较新词典与旧词典如何定义同一个词，考察新词典发生了怎样的改变。
Fortunately there is a way a way to recover some of that history. A very good place to start is with dictionaries. We can examine how newer dictionaries have changed by comparing their definitions of words, with how words were defined in older dictionaries.
诺亚·韦伯斯特Noah Webster编纂的 1828 年版《美国英语词典》，是英语史上最重要、最具影响力的词典之一，也是定义、词源、语言史与用法方面的杰作。《韦氏词典》历年经历多次改变；最后一个仍保留许多旧有词义与用法的版本，是 1913 年版。此后几乎所有词典都删去了早先用法的很大一部分。原书 p.217
The 1828 American Dictionary of the English Language by Noah Webster is one of the most important and influential English dictionaries ever compiled, and is a masterpiece of definition, etymology, language history and usage. The Webster dictionary went through numerous changes through the years, and the last edition to preserve much of the older language meaning and usage is the 1913 edition. Almost all later dictionaries have dropped much of the earlier usage.
1828 年版与 1913 年版《韦氏词典》都可以在线查阅。1828 年版还有纸质影印本，是一部奇妙迷人的作品。先翻阅 1828 年词典，再拿现代词典相比，就像从五星级美食餐厅走进麦当劳的得来速窗口。这个比喻很恰当：旧词典里的语言多样得多，辛香而滋味丰富；新词典里的语言则遭到削减，寡淡无味。
Both the 1828 and 1913 Webster dictionaries are available online. There is also a printed facsimile edition of the 1828 Webster available, and it is a marvelous and fascinating piece of work. Looking through the 1828 dictionary and then comparing a modern dictionary is like going from a 5 star gourmet restaurant to a MacDonald’s drive-through. That is a reasonable metaphor – language in the older dictionary is so much more varied, spicy and flavorful, and language in the new dictionary is stripped down and flavorless.
当语言受到削减，思想也受到削减，经验随之贫乏。在现代世界，我们生活在一种快餐式现实中。
When our language is stripped down, our thought is stripped down and our experience is impoverished. In the modern world we live in a fast-food reality.
与上面托马斯·泰勒的引文一样，这些旧词典指向一个大不相同的世界，也指向一个大不相同的 science 概念。这正是下面要考察的内容。
As with the quote from Thomas Taylor above, these old dictionaries point to a very different world, and a very different concept of science. That is what we will examine here.
以下引文出自《韦氏修订版无删节词典》（1913）。完整词条相当长，我只摘录一部分。所有引文中的粗体强调均为我所加。
These quotes are from Webster’s Revised Unabridged Dictionary (1913) – I am excerpting from the full definition of the term, which is quite long. Emphasis in bold in all quotes is mine.
1. 知识；关于本原与原因的知识；经过查明的事实真理。
1. Knowledge; knowledge of principles and causes; ascertained truth of facts.
这里还有两个重要词：本原与原因；二者的含义都随时间发生变化、变得狭窄。首先，principle 一词意为开端、起点、基础，即其他一切由之而来的东西。知道某物的本原，就是知道它的源头。cause 一词历年来同样发生巨大改变，词义缩减；在现代科学用法中，原因总是能够测量的物理事物。
There are two other important words here, principle and cause, both have which have changed and have shrunk in meaning over time. Firs, the word principle means beginning, starting point, basis that from which everything else follows. To know the principle of something is to know its source. The word cause has also greatly changed and reduced in meaning over the years – in modern scientific sense of the term cause is always something physical that can be measured.
在传统哲学中，亚里士多德列出四种不同的原因。现代科学只保留了动力因，也就是物理性的因果事件链。这里传统意义的「原因」，还带有亚里士多德形式因——赋予形态或形式之物——以及目的因——事物的终点或目的——的内涵。现代科学没有目的或意义的位置；缺少这一维度，占星学便毫无意义。原书 p.218
In traditional philosophy Aristotle laid out 4 different kinds of causes. Modern science has kept only the efficient cause, meaning the physical, cause and effect chain of events. Cause in the traditional sense here has connotations of Aristotle’s formal cause – that which gives the shape of form – and the final cause – its end or purpose. Modern science has no place for purpose or meaning, and without that dimension astrology is meaningless.
提示亚里士多德四因
四因为质料因、形式因、动力因与目的因。现代经验科学主要追踪可观察的动力因链条；作者强调，占星学还要求形式与目的的维度，因为它关心图式「意味着什么」，不只关心物理事件如何推动下一事件。
如果我们设想，在创造以前，上帝的视见或全知已经遍及世界整体及其每一部分，如实看见一切……那么，他自永恒以来的全知或视见，并不会给任何将发生之事施加必然性。——哈蒙德
If we conceive God’s sight or science, before the creation, to be extended to all and every part of the world, seeing everything as it is, . . . his science or sight from all eternity lays no necessity on anything to come to pass. –Hammond.
单是把上帝与 science 放进同一个句子，就告诉你我们身处另一个世界。把这与定义中的「本原」概念连起来，science 在这里就具有灵性意义，指向先于物质、其他一切由之涌出的终极实在。
Just using God and science in the same sentence tells you we’re living in a different world here. Link this to the concept of principle in the definition itself, and science is being used in a spiritual sense as pointing to the ultimate reality prior to the physical from which all else springs.
莎士比亚在心灵哲学方面深邃而准确的学问。——柯勒律治
Shakespeare’s deep and accurate science in mental philosophy. –Coleridge.
2. 累积并确立的知识，依发现一般真理或一般律法之运行而得到系统化与表述；经过分类、可用于工作、生活或寻求真理的知识；广博、深邃或哲学性的知识。
2. Accumulated and established knowledge, which has been systematized and formulated with reference to the discovery of general truths or the operation of general laws; knowledge classified and made available in work, life, or the search for truth; comprehensive, profound, or philosophical knowledge.
Science 是诸认识的完备集合；就形式而言，它具有逻辑完善的性质；就内容而言，它具有真实真理的性质。——威廉·汉密尔顿爵士
Science is . . . a complement of cognitions, having, in point of form, the character of logical perfection, and in point of matter, the character of real truth. – Sir W. Hamilton.
现在来看第三条定义。原书 p.219
And now we have definition number three.
3. 特别指这样的知识与物理世界及其现象、物质的本性、构成和力量、生命组织的性质与功能等相关时；——也称自然科学与物理科学。
3. Especially, such knowledge when it relates to the physical world and its phenomena, the nature, constitution, and forces of matter, the qualities and functions of living tissues, etc.; — called also {natural science}, and {physical science}.
在现代词典里，真正留下来的只有第三条定义。按当前用法，科学始于、也止于可测量的物理现象。它可以在实验室中测量，可以计算，可以在受控条件下精确重复——却没有意义、觉知、意识或价值的维度。
This third definition is really the only one left in the modern dictionary. In current usage science starts and ends with measurable physical phenomena. It can be measured in a lab, it can be calculated, it can be exactly replicated in controlled circumstances – and it has no dimension of meaning, or awareness, or consciousness, or value.
词条稍后还有一段，谈到 science 的不同维度。
A little bit later is this paragraph talking about the different dimensions of science.
注：Science 分为应用科学与纯粹科学。应用科学是关于事实、事件或现象的知识，这些事实、事件或现象借助力量、原因或律法得到解释、说明或产生。纯粹科学是关于这些力量、原因或律法自身的知识，将它们分别考察，或纯粹脱离一切应用而考察。这两个术语用于数量之科学时，具有类似而专门的含义，例如应用数学与纯粹数学。精密科学是经过系统化的知识，使通过测量、实验、观察等作出预测与验证成为可能。数学与物理科学被称为精密科学。
Note: Science is applied or pure. Applied science is a knowledge of facts, events, or phenomena, as explained, accounted for, or produced, by means of powers, causes, or laws. Pure science is the knowledge of these powers, causes, or laws, considered apart, or as pure from all applications. Both these terms have a similar and special signification when applied to the science of quantity; as, the applied and pure mathematics. Exact science is knowledge so systematized that prediction and verification, by measurement, experiment, observation, etc., are possible. The mathematical and physical sciences are called the exact sciences.
依我所见，我们在现代世界失去了一半含义——science 与 applied science 如今实际上已成同义词。韦氏所谓 pure science，已经从我们的用法中消失。
As far as I can tell we have lost half of the meaning in the modern world – science and applied science are now effectively synonyms. What Webster calls pure science has dropped out of our usage.
下面一段讲 science 一词的一种特殊用法，几乎与我们今天的用法正好相反。原书 p.220
In this next paragraph talks about a distinctive usage of the term science which is almost the inverse of the way we currently use the term.
用法：Science、Literature、Art。Science 的字面意义是知识，但更常指知识系统而有序的安排。更确切地说，science 包含这样一些知识分支：其题材或者是终极本原，或者是由本原或律法加以解释、从而依自然秩序排列的事实。
Usage: {Science}, {Literature}, {Art}. Science is literally knowledge, but more usually denotes a systematic and orderly arrangement of knowledge. In a more distinctive sense, science embraces those branches of knowledge of which the subject-matter is either ultimate principles, or facts as explained by principles or laws thus arranged in natural order.
Literature 一词有时指 science 未涵盖的一切文字作品，但通常只限于纯文学。〔见 Literature。〕Art 是依赖实践与操作技巧的东西。「在 science 中，我们求知是为了知识；在 art 中，我们求知是为了生产。」
The term literature sometimes denotes all compositions not embraced under science, but usually confined to the belles-lettres. [See {Literature}.] Art is that which depends on practice and skill in performance. “In science, scimus ut sciamus; in art, scimus ut producamus.
因此，science 与 art 都可说是对真理的探究；但 science 为知识而探究，art 为生产而探究；故 science 更关心较高真理，art 更关心较低真理；science 从不像 art 那样从事生产性应用。
And, therefore, science and art may be said to be investigations of truth; but one, science, inquires for the sake of knowledge; the other, art, for the sake of production; and hence science is more concerned with the higher truths, art with the lower; and science never is engaged, as art is, in productive application.
因此，science 最完善的状态将是最高、最准确的探究；art 的完善则是最合宜、最有效率的规则系统；art 总是把自身化为规则的形式。——卡斯莱克
And the most perfect state of science, therefore, will be the most high and accurate inquiry; the perfection of art will be the most apt and efficient system of rules; art always throwing itself into the form of rules.” –Karslake.
在这一系列段落中，science 与 art 的含义几乎与今天的用法颠倒。「关于终极本原的 science」已经消失。另一种「为了生产」的 science，实际上就是今天仅存的东西。science「更关心较高真理」的整个观念，在今天几乎不可思议。
In this series of paragraphs the meanings of science and art are very nearly reversed from their usage today. The ‘science of ultimate principles’ is gone. The other, science for ‘the sake of production’ is really all that is left today. The whole concept of science being ‘more concerned with the higher truths’ is pretty much unthinkable today.
校记“为了生产的 science”
按紧邻的词典引文，应是 art「为了生产」，science「为了知识」；原书本段却写成 science for “the sake of production”，上下文疑为笔误。中文正文保留原书逻辑表面，并在此标明。
现代科学中没有较高真理——因为除了物质现象世界，我们不承认还有一个较高世界能支撑这样的 science。没有那个较高世界，占星学就是毫无意义的迷信。这不只是语言变化，也是经验的贫乏，因为我们已经失去一些曾用来描述经验的概念之意义。原书 p.221
There are no higher truths in modern science – as there is no higher world than our material world of phenomena to support such a science. Without that higher world, astrology is meaningless superstition. This is not just change in language but an impoverishment of our experience because we have lost the meaning of some concepts used to describe it.
我认为，我们必须意识到现代语言的许多用法是多么贫乏，又怎样限制思想的可能性，这非常重要。在这个词缩减后的现代意义下，占星学这门「神圣学问」变成了一种非理性的迷信怪物，带上了「超自然」「通灵」等词的言外之意。占星学落在现代科学关于何为真实的规则之外。
I think it is very important that we become aware of just how impoverished much of our modern use of language is, and how it limits the possibilities of thinking. With the modern reduced meaning of the term, the “divine science” of astrology turns into an irrational superstitious oddity that has the connotations of words like supernatural and psychic. Astrology lies outside the modern scientific rules of what is real.
我见过一些非常优秀的现代占星师落入这个语言陷阱。他们迫切想为占星学辩护，声称它是这个词在现代还原论意义下的一门「科学」，于是急忙强调必须把占星学与「不过是」算命或灵能技艺区分开来。
I have seen some very fine modern astrologers fall into this linguistic trap. In their eagerness to defend astrology as a “science” in the modern reductionist sense of the term, they rush to emphasize how it is important to distinguish astrology from “mere” fortune-telling or psychic arts.
由于没有觉察 science 词义的改变，我认为我们在这里使用的是过于狭窄的意义；因此，占星实践与塔罗解读等灵能技艺确有亲缘的方式，就遭到我们贬低或忽略。
Because of the lack of awareness of the change in meaning of science, I think we are using science in too narrow a sense here, and so we are discounting or missing the ways in which the practice of astrology has real affinity with psychic arts like tarot reading.
这里作一项广告真实性声明：我本人解读塔罗牌和雷诺曼算命牌，无法在用牌所做的事与用占星学所做的事之间划出一条泾渭分明的界线。
Truth in advertising disclaimer here – I read tarot and Lenormand fortune-telling cards myself, and I can’t draw a hard and fast line between what I do with the cards and what I do with astrology.
从更广的意义说，占星实践不可能存在于语言与哲学的真空里，没有任何思想体系支撑。我们需要一种思考占星学的方式，使它变得有道理，并为它提供连贯的哲学基础。这就是本文的目的，也是我正在撰写的这一系列文章的主题。
In a larger sense, the practice of astrology can’t exist in a linguistic and philosophical vacuum, with no system of thought to support it. We need a way of thinking about astrology that makes sense of it, and that gives it a coherent philosophical underpinning. That is the purpose of this post, and is the main theme of the current series of posts I am writing.
如今我会说，占星学确实是一门 science；但我使用这个词的意义，与柏拉图主义传统中的托马斯·泰勒相同：它指向支撑我们在时空中存在的灵性本原、秩序与结构。占星学作为 science，的确可能有一个物理维度；但更重要的是，它具有灵性与哲学基础。原书 p.222
I now would say that astrology is indeed a science, but I am using the term in the same sense it is used by Thomas Taylor working within the Platonist tradition, as pointing to the spiritual principles, order and structure that underlies and upholds our existence in space and time. Astrology as a science may indeed have a physical dimension, but more importantly it has a spiritual and philosophical underpinning.
我并不轻视大卫·科克伦David Cochrane等占星师为占星学提供某种经验与统计论证的工作，但我不认为可以把占星学局限于那个领域。占星学太过宏大、面向太多，无法装进现代科学概念的边界。
I do not discount the work of astrologers like David Cochrane to give astrology some empirical and statistical justification, but I do not think we can limit astrology to that realm. Astrology is too big and multi-faceted to be contained within the bounds of the modern concept of science.
要成为有效的占星师、把自己的主张论证好，我们就必须思考清晰，必须弄清自己所用词语的含义；而当我们使用 science 这样的词，却采取日常话语中通常含义之外的意义时，就必须把这一点说得非常清楚明确。
If we are to be effective astrologers, and to argue our case well, we need to think clearly, we need to be clear about the meaning of the words we use – and, when we are using words like science outside of the usual connotations of the term in common discourse, we need to be very clear and explicit about that.
我们不只是在找回占星学，也在找回一个更丰富的世界观，以及占星学能在其中讲得通的语境——同时还在为我们的心智与现实找回一个神圣语境。
We are not just recovering astrology, we are also recovering a richer worldview and context within which astrology makes sense – and also recovering a divine context for our minds and our reality.

柏拉图与轮回：厄尔神话Plato and Reincarnation: the Myth of Er

柏拉图：灵魂的演化之旅Plato: The Evolutionary Journey of the Soul

人的灵魂来到地上有目的吗？我们在这里的生命，是否属于一种超越单独一世的更大经验？原书 p.223
Does the human soul have a purpose for being here on earth? Is our life here part of a larger experience that transcends a single human lifetime?
我从一位传统占星师的立场接近这个主题，而我的根基在柏拉图主义与毕达哥拉斯主义哲学传统。
I come at the subject as a traditional astrologer who is rooted in the Platonic and Pythagorean tradition of philosophy.
本文旨在考察柏拉图主义模型中的轮回与不朽灵魂，探索灵魂在生命中的目的，以及我们如何能在传统占星学中处理这个问题。
The purpose of this essay is to examine reincarnation and the immortal soul in a Platonist model - exploring the soul's purpose in life - and how we could address it in traditional astrology.

演化占星学Evolutionary Astrology

演化占星学Evolutionary astrology如今非常流行。它表现出现代占星学一个突出的面向：处理轮回与多世，并在这个语境中处理灵魂今生的目的。这是典型的现代进路，在谈论前世时高度强调外行星。与现代占星学的许多流派一样，演化占星学通常向印度寻求其工作的形而上学基础。
Evolutionary astrology is very popular today. It expresses a prominent dimension of modern astrology that works with reincarnation and multiple lives, and with the soul's purpose for this life within that context. This is a typically modern approach that heavily emphasizes the outer planets in talking about past lives. Like much modern astrology, evolutionary astrology typically looks to India for a metaphysical underpinning for their work.
人们渴望获得意义感与灵魂目的感；这正是吸引他们走向占星学的很大一部分原因。
That hunger for a sense of meaning and for a sense of soul purpose is a large part of what draws people to astrology.
我们能否利用西方传统占星学的语境与工具，处理演化占星学的主题——把不朽人类灵魂的一系列生命作为背景，考察灵魂今生的目的？我认为可以。
Can we address the subject matter of evolutionary astrology - the soul's purpose in this lifetime viewed in the context of a series of lives for the immortal human soul - using the context and tools of traditional western astrology? Yes, I think so.

柏拉图主义与毕达哥拉斯主义传统The Platonic and Pythagorean Tradition

我们自己的西方传统中就有一种轮回模型，其中包含多世，以及一个今生负有目的的不朽灵魂。我向柏拉图主义与毕达哥拉斯主义传统寻求这一语境。原书 p.224
In our own Western tradition we have a model of reincarnation, of multiple lives and an immortal soul having a purpose in this life. I look to the Platonic and Pythagorean tradition for that context.
我们完全可以非常有力地论证：柏拉图呈现的是一个不朽人类灵魂承担一系列人世生命的模型。我知道，现代学院研究中有一个显著学派声称，柏拉图并不「真正」相信轮回或不朽灵魂；他的对话录里所有相关说法都只是隐喻，不能当作本人的实际观点。
You can make a very strong case that Plato is presenting a model of an immortal human soul that takes on a series of human lives. I realize that there is a prominent school of modern academic scholarship which claims that Plato didn't "really" believe in reincarnation or an immortal soul, and that all of the references to those in his dialogs are metaphors and not to be taken as his actual views.
事实上，柏拉图在多篇对话录中对轮回与不朽灵魂有大量突出讨论。为了让他的对话录符合许多现代哲学的还原论立场，把「只是隐喻」的论点拉扯到这种程度，已经近乎荒谬。即便承认轮回与不朽灵魂的说法不该逐字照解，也应该认真对待：它们指向意识中一个超越单独一世边界的面向。
In fact, there is much prominent discussion of reincarnation and the immortal soul in Plato across several dialogs, and it is stretching the "only a metaphor" argument to a point of absurdity to make his dialogs agree with the reductionist stance of much modern philosophy. Granted that the references to reincarnation and the immortal soul are not to be taken literally, they are meant to be taken seriously as pointing to an aspect of consciousness that transcends the boundaries of a single earth life.
本文要考察柏拉图呈现多世模型最重要的段落之一，即《理想国》的结尾，通称「厄尔神话」。
In this post I want to examine one of the most important places where Plato presents a multi-lifetime model - the closing section of the Republic, known as The Myth of Er.
请注意，这里的「神话」一词意为记述、故事或叙事，并不带有我们今天附加给「神话般」一词的言外之意。柏拉图把它写成一名战士讲述的故事：此人被当作死者数日后复生，分享自己经历的一切。
Note that the word myth here means account or story or tale, and does not have the modern connotations we attach to the word mythical. Plato presents it as a tale told by a warrior who has been taken for dead for some days and returns to life to share what he experienced.
术语mythos 不是“虚构谎言”
这里的「神话」承接希腊语 mythos 的叙事、讲述之义。作者并未要求把细节当作可验证报道，而是反对因现代「不真实」的含义，预先取消这则故事对死后存在与灵魂多世旅程的严肃指向。
我想把这一段放回全书论证的语境，因为这个故事正是整部作品的顶点。
I want to put the section in the context of the argument of the entire book, as this story is really the culmination.

正义通向幸福，不义通向苦难Justice leads to Happiness, Injustice to Suffering

行事正义值得吗？正义的人生也会幸福、成功吗？还是说，以不义方式行事有时反而能取得好处？原书 p.225
Does it pay to act justly? Is the just life also the happy and successful life? Or, does it sometimes pay to act in unjust ways to gain advantage?
这是整篇对话开篇的问题，也是把全书维系在一起的线索。苏格拉底从一开始就试图证明：正义而有德性地行动，本身就有益；不义地行动，本身就有害；以不义方式行事绝不会带来益处——为了获益而行不义，是自相矛盾、弄巧成拙。
This is the question that starts the entire dialog, and is the thread that holds the entire book together. From the very start Socrates is attempting to prove that acting justly and virtuously is beneficial in its own right, acting unjustly is harmful in its own right, and that it is never of benefit to act unjustly - that acting unjustly for benefit is self-contradictory and self-defeating.
本书很大一部分涉及德性是什么的定义，以及有德性地行动是否总是有利，或不义行为能否产生好结果。它在政治结构的语境中考察正义与德性，也在与之平行的个体人类灵魂语境中考察。个体结构与政治结构彼此映照；城邦中的有德性行动，与人类灵魂内部有德性的平衡相映照。个体秩序与政治秩序，又都映照着我们在占星学中研究的更大宇宙结构。
Much of the book has to do with the definition of what virtue is, and whether to act virtuously is always advantageous, or whether to act in unjust ways can produce good results. It examines justice and virtue in the context of a political structure, and in the parallel context of the individual human soul. The individual and political structures mirror each other, and virtuous action in a city is mirrored by the virtuous balance within the human soul. Both the individual and the political order are mirrors of the larger cosmic structure that we study in astrology.

两个世界The Two Worlds

在柏拉图模型中有两个世界、两个存在维度。一是超越时空的永恒理型世界；一是物理存在、身体、感官与激情所属的时间性、变易而短暂的世界。
In Plato's model there are two worlds, two dimensions of existence. There is the eternal world of the Forms, which transcends time and space. There is also the temporal, changing, transitory world of physical existence, of the body, the senses and passions.
人类意识有两个面向，分别映照这两个世界。一是不朽灵魂，一是与人类身体相系、可朽而短暂的人类灵魂；身体与灵魂的短暂部分都会在死亡时分解。
There are two aspects that human consciousness that mirror these two worlds. There is the immortal soul, and there is a mortal and temporary human soul tied to the human body, and both the body and the transitory part of the soul disintegrate at death.
柏拉图与亚里士多德都把不朽灵魂称作智性（Nous）Nous，这个词意为心灵。它是人身上思考、判断与推理的部分，比我们今天的理性概念更广大。在柏拉图模型中，理性带有底层本原的内涵，并包括分辨对错的能力。永恒世界的基本结构本身就具有道德与伦理维度。
Both Plato and Aristotle identify the immortal soul as the Nous, a word that means Mind. It is the thinking, judging and reasoning part of the human. This is larger than our current concept of reason. In Plato's model reason has a connotation of underlying principles, and includes the ability to distinguish right from wrong. There is a moral and ethical dimension that is part of the basic structure of the eternal world.

不朽灵魂The Immortal Soul

这个故事承接第 608 节的一段论述，苏格拉底在那里试图证明灵魂必定不朽。原书 p.226
The story follows on a section [608] where Socrates attempts to prove that the soul must be immortal.
灵魂不朽的论证，基本上是一项从正义出发的论证。苏格拉底主张，唯一能够伤害灵魂的，是不义或违背德性。这意味着，能够伤害或杀死身体的东西，未必会伤害灵魂。
The argument for the soul's immortality is basically an argument from justice. Socrates argues that the only thing that can harm the soul is injustice or going against virtue. This means that whatever can harm or kill the body does not necessarily harm the soul.
然而，违背德性或正义的行动并不会杀死灵魂，只会创伤灵魂、造成苦难。事实上，唯一能伤害灵魂的，就是违背正义与德性而行。不义会伤害灵魂，却不摧毁它。若唯一能伤害灵魂的事物不能杀死它，那么在一个正义的宇宙里，灵魂必定不朽。
However, acting against virtue or justice does not kill the soul, but does wound it and cause suffering. In fact the only thing that can harm the soul is acting against justice and virtue. Injustice harms the soul but does not destroy it. If the only thing that can harm the soul does not kill it, then the soul must be immortal in a just universe.
只有我们化身于物质的部分受制于可朽性、死亡与毁灭。我们的不朽智性，我们具有感知、推理与判断能力的心灵，无法被摧毁。
Only the physical incarnate part of us is subject to mortality, to death and destruction. Our immortal Nous, our perceiving, reasoning and judging mind, cannot be destroyed.
智性即不朽灵魂的根基，与宇宙本身的根基一样，是至善——德性与正义。宇宙的本质与根本本原，即太一与至善，是不朽而正义的；或者更准确地说，既然正义从太一与至善中涌现，它的源头就必定包含并超越正义。
The basis of the Nous, the immortal soul, like the basis of the universe itself, is The Good - virtue, justice. The essence, the base principle of the universe, the One and the Good, is immortal and just - or rather, since justice emerges from the One and the Good then its source must contain and transcend justice.
在柏拉图模型中，我们生活在一个正义的宇宙、道德的宇宙；正义的本原一路抵达存在的核心。
In the Platonic model we live in a just universe, a moral universe, where the principles of justice reach all the way to the core of being.
这是整个论证的关键本原：人身上真实、永恒的部分，即不朽灵魂，正是那个对德性与恶习、正义与不义作出决定的部分。因此，它才是我们值得培育的部分；哲学生活的全部意义，就在于培育这种觉知，学习使它成为我们一切行动的引导之光。
This is the key principle of the entire argument - that which is real, that which eternal in the human, the immortal soul, is that part of us which makes the decisions about virtue and vice, justice and injustice. Therefore that is the part of us that is worth cultivating - and that is what the philosophic life is all about - cultivating that awareness, and learning to make it the guiding light of all of our actions.
请记住这个本原。本文结尾把这篇对话录同占星实践联系起来时，我们还会回到这里。原书 p.227
Keep that principle in mind. We will return to it at the end of this essay, when we tie in how this dialog applies to our practice of astrology.

一生太短，不足以实现正义One Lifetime Too Short for Justice

既然宇宙正义，而人的灵魂不朽，那么每个灵魂就必定拥有不止一段可朽生命，因为单独一生的时间太短，不足以让善恶问题得到充分展开与清算。
Given that the universe is just and the human soul is immortal, it follows that each soul must have more than one mortal life, as one lifetime is too short a length of time to fully work out issues of good and evil.
这个最后的故事涉及不朽灵魂、死后世界，以及我们在两世之间选择下一世的阶段，旨在把整套论证联结起来。苏格拉底已经论证，正义自有其奖赏，本身就值得；不义自有其惩罚，会制造自身的问题。以不义方式行事绝不会有益。
This final tale, on the immortal soul, the after life, and the period between where we choose our next life, is designed to tie the entire argument together. Socrates has argued that justice is its own reward and is worthwhile in itself, and injustice is its own punishment and creates its own problems. It is never of benefit to act in an unjust way.
如今我们已经在一生的跨度内确立这一原则；这个故事进而把它放入不朽灵魂横越多世的朝圣旅程，以及各世之间奖赏期或惩罚期的语境。
Now that we have that principle in place within the span of a single lifetime, the tale puts it in context of the pilgrimage of the immortal soul across multiple lifetimes, and the periods of reward or punishment between.
请注意，本文所有柏拉图引文都采用已进入公版的本杰明·乔伊特Benjamin Jowett译本，我参照康福德译本改动了少数段落，使其更清楚。引文在原文中使用斜体，我还强调了一些格外重要的文句。方括号内数字是柏拉图对话录的标准斯特凡努斯页码，所有译本都会引用。在解释方面，我尤其受益于 F. M. 康福德的《理想国》译注。
Note that all quotes from Plato here are from the public domain Benjamin Jowett translation, with a few sections altered for clarity by me based on the Cornford translation. Quotes are in italics, and I have emphasized some particularly important passages. The numbers in square braces are the standard section numbers for Plato's dialogs, that are referenced in all translations. For the interpretation I am particularly indebted to the translation and commentary of the Republic by F. M. Cornford.

死而复返Returning from the Dead

死后世界的故事由此过渡展开。
The transition to the story of the afterlife begins here.
「那么，这些就是正义之人在世时，从神与人那里得到的奖品、报酬与礼物〔614a〕，还不包括正义本身赐予的福分。」
「那些奖赏确实美好而长久。」他说。原书 p.228
“Such then while he lives are the prizes, the wages, and the gifts [614a] that the just man receives from gods and men in addition to those blessings which justice herself bestowed.” “And right fair and abiding rewards,” he said.
「好吧，」我说，「与死后等待正义者和不义者的奖赏相比，这些无论数量还是大小都算不得什么。我们必须听一听那些事的故事，」我说，「好让我们的论证完整交代两者各自应得的一切。」
Well, these,” I said, “are nothing in number and magnitude compared with those that await both after death. And we must listen to the tale of them,” said I, “in order that each may have received in full what is due to be said of him by our argument.”
「请讲吧，」他说，〔614b〕「再没有多少事情比这更令我乐于倾听。」
“Tell me,” he said, [614b] “since there are not many things to which I would more gladly listen.”
「我要讲给你的，」我说，「不是讲给阿尔基诺奥斯的故事，而是一位勇敢战士的故事：他叫厄尔，是阿尔墨尼俄斯之子，族属潘菲利亚人。
“It is not, let me tell you,” said I, “the tale to Alcinous told that I shall unfold, but the tale of a warrior bold, Er, the son of Armenius, by race a Pamphylian.
从前他在战斗中阵亡。第十天收殓已经腐烂的尸体时，人们发现他的身体完好无损，于是带回家中。第十二天举行葬礼，他躺在柴堆上，忽然复活；苏醒后，他讲述自己声称在彼岸世界看见的一切。」
He once upon a time was slain in battle, and when the corpses were taken up on the tenth day already decayed, was found intact, and having been brought home, at the moment of his funeral, on the twelfth day as he lay upon the pyre, revived, and after coming to life related what, he said, he had seen in the world beyond.
这被写成一个曾在死后世界停留、继而复生之人讲述的故事。它没有被描述成童话或虚构神话，而是一篇记述。该如何理解，由读者决定。即便承认细节可能并非实际描述——但如果这是隐喻，又是什么的隐喻？它确实指向身体死后仍然延续的存在；至少，这个故事是指向那种存在之重要面向的一种方式。
This is presented as a story told by a man who returned to life after being in the world after death. It is not described as a fairy tale or myth, but an account. How it is to be taken depends on the reader. Granted that the details may not be meant as actual description - but if it is a metaphor, then a metaphor for what? It is definitely pointing to a continued existence after death of the body, and the story at its very least is a way to point to important aspects of that existence.
如果它是隐喻，就确实指向某种横跨多世的持续存在，即便柏拉图在这里为它设置的细节并非意在照字面理解。他是在指向它，而不是把它钉死。
If it is metaphor it definitely points to some form of continued existence over multiple lives, even if the details he frames it in here are not meant to be literal. He is pointing to it rather than pinning it down.

天堂与地狱Heaven and Hell

他说，灵魂离开身体后，便与一大群灵魂同行〔614c〕，来到一处神秘地方。那里地上并排有两个洞口，在它们上方相对的天上也有两个洞口；审判者坐在天地洞口之间。每次审判后，他们命正义者从右边向上，经由天上的道路前行，胸前系着标明判决的记号；又命不义者从左边向下，背后也带着记载自己所有遭遇的标记〔614d〕。他本人走近时，审判者告诉他，他必须成为前往人间的信使，把彼岸世界告诉人类；他们嘱咐他倾听并观察当地的一切。原书 p.229
He said that when his soul went forth from his body he journeyed with a great company [614c] and that they came to a mysterious region where there were two openings side by side in the earth, and above and over against them in the heaven two others, and that judges were sitting between these, and that after every judgment they bade the righteous journey to the right and upwards through the heaven with tokens attached to them in front of the judgment passed upon them, and the unjust to take the road to the left and downward, they too wearing behind signs [614d] of all that had befallen them, and that when he himself drew near they told him that he must be the messenger to mankind to tell them of that other world, and they charged him to give ear and to observe everything in the place.
于是他说，自己在那里看见：天上与地下各有一个洞口，灵魂受审后分别从中离去；另外两个洞口中，一个有灵魂从地下升起，满身污垢尘土，另一个则有第二队灵魂从天而降，洁净纯粹〔614e〕。不时抵达的灵魂看起来仿佛经历了长途旅行；他们欣然走到草地，像参加节庆一样在那里扎营。相识者彼此问候；从地下来的询问上方的情形，从天上来的则询问下方的遭遇。
And so he said that here he saw, by each opening of heaven and earth, the souls departing after judgment had been passed upon them, while, by the other pair of openings, there came up from the one in the earth souls full of squalor and dust, and from the second there came down from heaven a second procession of souls clean and pure, [614e] and that those which arrived from time to time appeared to have come as it were from a long journey and gladly departed to the meadow and encamped there as at a festival, and acquaintances greeted one another, and those which came from the earth questioned the others about conditions up yonder, and those from heaven asked how it fared with those others.
他们彼此讲述自己的故事。从地下来的回想地底旅程中遭受、目睹的众多可怕之事，哀恸哭号〔615a〕——旅程持续了一千年；从天上来的则讲述自己的喜悦，以及无法用语言形容的美景。
And they told their stories to one another, the one lamenting [615a] and wailing as they recalled how many and how dreadful things they had suffered and seen in their journey beneath the earth - it lasted a thousand years --while those from heaven related their delights and visions of a beauty beyond words.
《理想国》的总体论证是，有德性地行动值得。这个部分开头，苏格拉底基本上说，即便只在今生的语境里也会得到回报；这个故事则把它放进多世语境，包括每一世之间长达千年的天堂或地狱——也就是说，无论种下善或恶，我们都将收获十倍。原书 p.230
The general argument of the Republic is that it pays to act virtuously - at the start of this section he basically says it pays off even in the context of this one life - and this tale puts it in the multi life context including a thousand years of either heaven or hell in between each life - meaning we reap tenfold of what we sow of either good or evil.
死后的奖赏或惩罚，与一个人多么富有、成功、著名、有钱、受人喜爱或其他任何外在条件都毫无关系。标准纯属伦理：你的一生正义还是不义。请留意，只有两者之一：要么完全向上，要么完全向下；死后生命要么是纯粹福乐，要么是纯粹苦难，中间什么也没有。
Reward or punishment after death has nothing to do with how rich, or successful, or famous, or wealthy, or well-liked, or whatever. The criteria are strictly ethical - did you live justly or unjustly. And notice it is either one or the other, completely up or completely down, an after life of unalloyed bliss or unalloyed suffering, with nothing in-between.
这支持隐喻性的读法；但这个隐喻强烈指向一项事实：我们所有行动都会在正义与不义方面产生后果——我们收获正义而有德性之行动的奖赏，也收获不义行动带来的苦难与恶果。这个宇宙以正义和不义为核心结构本原，处于存在全部意义的正中心。
That argues for this being metaphorical - but then the metaphor is strongly pointing to the fact that all our actions have consequences in terms of justice and injustice - that we reap the rewards of just and virtuous action, and that we reap the suffering and ill fruit of unjust action. It is a universe where justice and injustice are core structural principles, at the very heart of what existence is all about.
这里令我震动的是，它的黑白二元性如此强烈——你要么在左，要么在右；要么恶，要么善；要么向下，要么向上；中间什么也没有。显而易见，基督宗教的天堂地狱要么以此为模型，要么与它追溯到共同源头。
What strikes me here is how very black and white dualistic it is - you are either left or right, evil or good, down or up, and there is nothing in-between. It is pretty obvious that either the Christian heaven and hell are modeled after this or they go back to a common root.
在这个故事中，尽管每次两世之间的状态要么是纯粹天堂，要么是纯粹地狱，但每一世之间都有重新选择、继续成长并向前行进的机会。
In this story, while each in-between life is either a sheer heaven or a sheer hell, between each life there is the chance to choose anew, to keep growing, to move on.
天堂或地狱两种状态都不是永久的。从后面的段落可以看到，与单独一世之内一样，人类的一般倾向是迅速遗忘。从天堂返回者容易忘掉教训，作出糟糕选择；从地狱返回者因为通过苦难学到了东西，反而倾向于作出更明智的选择。
Neither condition, of either heaven or hell, is permanent. From later sections here we will see that, as within a single human life, the general human tendency is to forget quickly. Those returning from heaven tend to forget their lessons and choose poorly. Those returning from hell tend to choose more wisely since they have learned through suffering.
就单独一生而言，这暗示好运在伦理上其实更加危险，而不幸与苦难才是灵魂成长的最佳环境。换言之，我们的德性在逆境的土壤里生长得最好。
In terms of a single life this implies that good fortune is actually more dangerous ethically, while misfortune and suffering is the best environment for growing the soul. Or virtues grow best in a soil of adversity.
柏拉图主张，一般而言，我们在生活中作出选择，是出于惯性与习惯，而不是有意识的伦理选择。《理想国》以及柏拉图所有对话录都在论证：有意识的伦理选择才是唯一重要之事——事实上，它最终是唯一真实而永恒之事。原书 p.231
Plato argues that, in general, we make our choices in life out of inertia and habit rather than conscious ethical choice. The Republic, and all of the Platonic dialogs, are arguing for conscious ethical choice as being the only thing that matters - indeed, as ultimately being the only thing that is real and eternal.
格劳孔，要把一切都讲完，会耗尽我们所有时间；但他所说的大意如下。他们曾对任何人犯下的一切过错，以及他们各自伤害过的所有人，每一项都依次付出十倍惩罚；衡量方式是每一百年为一期〔615b〕，把百年当作人的一生，惩罚就达到罪行的十倍。
To tell it all, Glaucon, would take all our time, but the sum, he said, was this. For all the wrongs they had ever done to anyone and all whom they had severally wronged they had paid the penalty in turn tenfold for each, and the measure of this was by periods of a hundred years each [615b] so that on the assumption that this was the length of human life the punishment might be ten times the crime;

为下一世作准备Getting Ready for the Next Life

……然而，每一群灵魂在草地停留七天后，到了第八天便必须起身继续旅程。四天后，他们来到一个地点，从那里看见一道笔直的光柱自上而下延伸，贯穿天与地；它最像彩虹，却更加明亮纯净。
...But when seven days had elapsed for each group in the meadow, they were required to rise up on the eighth and journey on, and they came in four days to a spot whence they discerned, extended from above throughout the heaven and the earth, a straight light like a pillar, most nearly resembling the rainbow, but brighter and purer.
这片平原，是返回的灵魂为新的尘世生命作准备时前往的地方；无论他们先前所在的是地狱还是天堂，每一个灵魂都要作出这样的选择。
This plain is a place where returning souls go when they are getting ready for a new earth life - and that is the choice of each regardless of whether or not their previous place was hell or heaven.

必然之纺锤The Spindle of Necessity

他们又前行一日后抵达那道光〔616c〕，看见光带中央有它从天上延伸下来的系结端；因为这道光是诸天的束带，像三列桨战船船底的系缆一样，以同样方式维系整个旋转的穹苍。必然之纺锤从这些端点延伸，所有轨道都由它转动。纺锤的轴杆与钩子由精金刚石制成，锤轮则混合这种材料与其他材料制成。
To this they came [616c] after going forward a day's journey, and they saw there at the middle of the light the extremities of its fastenings stretched from heaven; for this light was the girdle of the heavens like the undergirders of triremes, holding together in like manner the entire revolving vault. And from the extremities was stretched the spindle of Necessity, through which all the orbits turned. Its staff and its hook were made of adamant, and the whorl of these and other kinds was commingled.
柏拉图在这里描述的模型——他称之为必然之纺锤——就是地心太阳系，是一套围绕纺锤旋转的天球系统。从我们的观点看，那根纺锤就是赤道及其周日旋转；那就是时钟，是把一切联结在一起的主要秩序系统。占星学的世界由此进入。原书 p.232
The model that Plato describes here, which he calls the Spindle of Necessity, is the geocentric solar system, a system of spheres revolving around a spindle. From our point of view that spindle is the equator and its daily revolution - that is the clock, the main system of order that ties everything together. This is where the world of astrology comes in.
因此，必然之纺锤周围的那组天球，就是轮回灵魂为一段尘世生命而进入的领域，也是命运三女神所掌管的领域。
So that set of spheres around the Spindle of Necessity is the domain that the reincarnating soul enters for an earth life. That is the domain controlled by the three fates.
这暗示，两世之间更大的领域或结构位于必然之纺锤以外；然而整个宇宙都以正义的本原为根基、依其构成。换言之，整个宇宙——物质与非物质、灵性与物理、永恒与时间——赖以建立的结构性根本本原，就是正义、美、慈悲与爱的永恒本原。按字面说，没有这些本原，宇宙就没有秩序或理性；说得更好一点，没有这些本原，就没有宇宙。
That implies that the larger between life domain or structure is outside of that spindle of necessity - but that the entire universe is founded, structured, on principles of justice - in other words that the very structural foundational principles or structures that the entire universe is built on, material and immaterial, spiritual and physical, eternal and temporal - is the eternal principles of justice, of beauty, of mercy and love. Literally, that without those principles the universe has no order or reason - or better yet, that without those principles there is no universe.
必然之纺锤就是南北极轴，各个锤轮或天球则是行星天球加恒星天球。因此，化身就是进入太阳系，进入占星诸神的世界。整个系统是命运三女神的领域；化身则意味着从群星下降，穿过层层天球，一路降至地上。
The spindle of necessity is the north/south pole, and the whorls or orbs are the planetary spheres plus the fixed stars. So incarnation is entering into the solar system, the world of the gods of astrology. This whole system is the sphere of the fates, and incarnation consists of descending out of the stars and through the spheres and down into earth.
锤轮的本性如下：〔616d〕它的形状与我们世上的锤轮一样，但依照他的描述，我们必须设想一个巨大、中空而内凹的锤轮，其中从头到尾套着另一个同类却较小的锤轮，彼此嵌合如套在一起的盒子；同样又套着第三个、第四个，以及另外四个。总共有八个锤轮层层相套〔616e〕；从上方看，它们的边缘显为圆环，并围绕贯穿第八个锤轮中央的轴杆，共同构成一个锤轮连续的背面。
And the nature of the whorl was this: [616d] Its shape was that of those in our world, but from his description we must conceive it to be as if in one great whorl, hollow and scooped out, there lay enclosed, right through, another like it but smaller, fitting into it as boxes that fit into one another, and in like manner another, a third, and a fourth, and four others, for there were eight of the whorls in all, lying within one another, [616e] showing their rims as circles from above and forming the continuous back of a single whorl about the shaft, which was driven home through the middle of the eighth.
纺锤在必然女神膝上转动；每个圆环的边缘上方都站着一位塞壬，随着圆环旋转，发出一个声音、一个音符；八个声音共同和鸣成单一和声。原书 p.233
And the spindle turned on the knees of Necessity, and up above on each of the rims of the circles a Siren stood, borne around in its revolution and uttering one sound, one note, and from all the eight there was the concord of a single harmony.
这就是天球音乐——所有行星的旋转共同奏出和声。
The music of the spheres - all of the planetary rotations together sounding a harmony.
提示八个锤轮
八个相套锤轮对应恒星天球与古典七曜的行星天球；天极轴贯穿中央，整体作周日旋转。每一轮的塞壬各发一音，合成「天球音乐」；这使灵魂投生所进入的占星宇宙同时呈现为秩序、时间与和声。

命运三女神The Three Fates

还有另外三位〔617c〕，等距环坐，各居宝座；她们是命运三女神、必然女神的女儿，身穿白袍，头缠饰带，名叫拉刻西斯、克洛托与阿特洛波斯。她们与塞壬的音乐齐声歌唱：拉刻西斯歌唱过去之事，克洛托歌唱现在之事，阿特洛波斯歌唱未来之事。
And there were another three [617c] who sat round about at equal intervals, each one on her throne, the Fates, 1 daughters of Necessity, clad in white vestments with filleted heads, Lachesis, and Clotho, and Atropos, who sang in unison with the music of the Sirens, Lachesis singing the things that were, Clotho the things that are, and Atropos the things that are to be.
这就是命运三女神——所以她们位于整个系统之外，不能等同于任何一颗行星。而且，命运三女神的领域就是地上生命；本节稍后清楚说明，每个灵魂都自由选择自己即将到来的生命——可能不经思考，也可能深思熟虑。
These are the three Fates - so they are outside of the entire system, not to be identified with any one planet. And also, the domain of the Fates is life on earth - and a bit later in this section it is made clear that each soul freely chooses its coming lifetime - either thoughtlessly or thoughtfully.
命运三女神是构成过去、现在与未来世界的力量；她们织出经纬与图式，组成我们所知的化身人类存在。
The fates are those powers that structure the world of past, present and future - that weave the web and pattern that makes up the incarnate human existence that we know.
无论如何，选择都是依欲望与价值作出的——灵魂得到自己强烈渴求的生命。
One way or other it is chosen in terms of desire and value - the soul gets the life it is lusting after.
克洛托以右手触碰，帮助转动纺锤外缘，时而停歇；阿特洛波斯同样用左手帮助转动内侧圆环；拉刻西斯〔617d〕则左右手交替，协助转动两者。原书 p.234
And Clotho with the touch of her right hand helped to turn the outer circumference of the spindle, pausing from time to time. Atropos with her left hand in like manner helped to turn the inner circles, and Lachesis [617d] alternately with either hand lent a hand to each.
「他们抵达后，立刻被命令走到拉刻西斯面前。随后，一位先知首先把他们排成间隔整齐的队伍，继而从拉刻西斯膝上取过生命之签与生命范型，登上高台说道：
“Now when they arrived they were straight-way bidden to go before Lachesis, and then a certain prophet first marshaled them in orderly intervals, and thereupon took from the lap of Lachesis lots and patterns of lives and went up to a lofty platform and spoke,
『这是必然女神年轻女儿拉刻西斯的宣告：「一日之灵魂啊，如今又一个可朽世代的周期开始了，在那里，出生就是死亡的信标。〔617e〕不会有神明替你们抽签；你们要选择自己的守护神。抽中第一签者，先选择一段生命，此后他将受必然约束，依附于它。
‘This is the word of Lachesis, the maiden daughter of Necessity, “Souls that live for a day, now is the beginning of another cycle of mortal generation where birth is the beacon of death. [617e] No divinity shall cast lots for you, but you shall choose your own deity. Let him to whom falls the first lot first select a life to which he shall cleave of necessity.
但德性没有主人；每个人尊崇她或轻侮她，便相应多得或少得她。责任在选择者；上帝无可归咎。」』
But virtue has no master over her, and each shall have more or less of her as he honors her or does her despite. The blame is his who chooses: God is blameless. "

人生由我们负责——不归咎他者Our Life is Our Responsibility - There is no Blame

每个灵魂选择自己的生命之签，也就是它想要的人生大致形态，以及将得到的挑战。上帝无可归咎；每个灵魂都要为自己的签负责。从根本上说，这意味着我们选择自己的命定。
Each soul chooses its lot in life, the general shape of the life it wants and the challenges it gets. God is blameless; each is responsible for their own lot. Essentially this means that we choose our own fate.
我们依照自己所重视之物选择生命，并收获那些价值选择的后果。从根本上说，我们选择想从一生中得到什么，继而收获这些选择的后果。
We choose our lives in terms of what we value, and we reap the consequences of those value choices. Essentially we choose what we want out of the life; we then reap the consequences of those choices.
提示神话中的投生选择不等于责怪受苦者
这里的「负责」发生在神话所设定的投生前层次，并不表示今生遭遇疾病、事故或暴力的人在日常人格层面「活该」。作者第 182–196 页明确反对在咨询中如此归责；本节真正强调的是：外在命运既定之后，灵魂仍须为自己的正义或不义回应负责。
先知说完，便把签抛向所有灵魂；除了厄尔——他们不准他抽签——每个灵魂都捡起落在自己身旁的签。拿到签的人，都清楚看见自己抽中了什么号码。
So saying, the prophet flung the lots out among them all, and each took up the lot that fell by his side, except himself; him they did not permit . And whoever took up a lot saw plainly what number he had drawn.
〔618a〕随后，先知又把生命范型放在他们面前的地上，数量远比在场灵魂更多。它们种类齐全，有各种动物的生命，也有各种人的生命。其中有僭主的一生：有些统治不间断直到终结，另一些在中途遭到毁灭，最后陷入贫困、流亡与乞讨；也有声名卓著之人的生命，他们以容貌美丽、身体强健〔618b〕、英勇、高贵出身与祖先德性著称；还有在这些方面声名不佳者的生命，女性亦同。原书 p.235
[618a] And after this again the prophet placed the patterns of lives before them on the ground, far more numerous than the assembly. They were of every variety, for there were lives of all kinds of animals and all sorts of human lives, for there were tyrannies among them, some uninterrupted till the end and others destroyed midway and issuing in penuries and exiles and beggaries; and there were lives of men of repute for their forms and beauty and bodily strength otherwise [618b] and prowess and the high birth and the virtues of their ancestors, and others of ill repute in the same things, and similarly of women.
但灵魂的品质并没有预先决定，因为选择不同生命，必然会决定不同的品格。
But there was no determination of the quality of soul, because the choice of a different life inevitably determined a different character.
这里非常重要：一生的好运或坏运，与灵魂品质的决定毫无关系。这意味着，依照正义与德性，好运坏运都可能被用来成善，也可能被用来成恶。（在占星学中运用吉星与凶星——好运与坏运的行星——时，这一点极其重要。吉星与凶星同伦理意义上的善恶毫无关系。）
Very important here - the good or bad fortune of the life has nothing to do with the determination of quality of soul. That means good or bad fortune can be turned to good or bad account according to justice and virtue. (This is very important in astrology when we work with the benefics and malefics, the planets of good and ill fortune. Benefic and malefic has nothing to do with good and evil in any ethical sense.)
在更高维度，这暗示已经有一组预先安排的生命等待选择、等待演出，也暗示每一段人的生命都有其位置。生命的结构就像一出戏，主要情节与角色已经确定，如今所有演员正在戏中选择自己的角色。
At a higher dimension, this implies that there is an already planned set of lives to be chosen from, to be enacted. This implies that each human life has a place. Life is structured like a play where the main plot and its characters are set, and now the actors are all selecting their part within the play.
但其他一切彼此混合：财富与贫穷、疾病与健康，以及各种中间状态。——亲爱的格劳孔，人的最大危险似乎正在这里。
But all other things were commingled with one another and with wealth and poverty and sickness and health and the intermediate conditions. - And there, dear Glaucon, it appears, is the supreme hazard for a man.
〔618c〕这正是为什么，我们最主要的关切应当是：每个人都放下其他一切学问，寻求并研习这一件事——只要可能，就要学会并找到一个人，使自己获得能力与知识，分辨善好的人生与恶劣的人生，无论何时何地，总能选择条件所容许的最佳人生；同时考虑我们谈过的一切，评估它们结合或分离时怎样影响一生的善好，认识美与贫穷或财富混合，再与灵魂怎样的习性结合时〔618d〕，会产生善或恶。原书 p.236
[618c] And this is the chief reason why it should be our main concern that each of us, neglecting all other studies, should seek after and study this thing - if in any way he may be able to learn of and discover the man who will give him the ability and the knowledge to distinguish the life that is good from that which is bad, and always and everywhere to choose the best that the conditions allow, and, taking into account all the things of which we have spoken and estimating the effect on the goodness of his life of their conjunction or their severance, to know how beauty commingled with poverty or wealth and combined with [618d] what habit of soul operates for good or for evil,
值得强调的是，好运坏运与善恶是两个完全不同的范畴。我们抵达这里以后，无法控制命运的大致形态——在这个模型中，我们先前已经选择了自己的人生之签——但我们永远能够控制灵魂的品质，控制以善或恶回应；而决定我们如何接受审判的，恰恰是这种回应。
It is worth emphasizing that good and bad fortune, and good and evil, are two completely different categories. We do not have control over the general shape of fortune once we get here - in this model we have already previously chosen our lot in life - but we always have control over quality of soul, over good and evil response - and it is precisely that response which determines how we are judged.
还要认识高贵或卑微出身、平民身份或公职、强健或虚弱、敏锐领悟或迟钝，以及灵魂所有类似的天生与后天习性，在彼此混合结合时会产生什么效果。
and what are the effects of high and low birth and private station and office and strength and weakness and quickness of apprehension and dullness and all similar natural and acquired habits of the soul, when blended and combined with one another,
如此，考虑这一切之后，他就能在较善与较恶的人生之间作出理性选择〔618e〕，眼睛始终注视自己灵魂的本性：会使灵魂变得更不义的人生叫作较恶，会使它变得更正义的人生叫作较善。
so that with consideration of all these things he will be able to make a reasoned choice between the better and the worse life, [618e] with his eyes fixed on the nature of his soul, naming the worse life that which will tend to make it more unjust and the better that which will make it more just.
这就是核心——《理想国》的主要论点，也是人生的主要理由：增强我们作出有意识而明智的价值判断之能力。
This is the core - the main argument of the Republic - and the main reason for human life - is to grow in our ability to make conscious and wise value judgments.
但他会抛开所有其他考虑，因为我们已经看出，无论对生还是对死，这都是最佳选择〔619a〕。人走进死亡之家时，必须带着对此坚如金刚的信念，才不会在那里被财富及类似的虚饰迷花眼睛，一头投入僭主及类似人生，造下许多无可挽回的恶，自己又承受更大苦难；他必须懂得，始终在这类事物中选择处于中道的人生，避开两边的过度——在今生尽可能如此，在所有未来生命中也如此〔619b〕；因为这就是人最大的幸福。原书 p.237
But all other considerations he will dismiss, for we have seen that this is the best choice, [619a] both for life and death. And a man must take with him to the house of death an adamantine faith in this, that even there he may be undazzled by riches and similar trumpery, and may not precipitate himself into tyrannies and similar doings and so work many evils past cure and suffer still greater himself, but may know how always to choose in such things the life that is seated in the mean and shun the excess in either direction, both in this world so far as may be and in all the life to come; [619b] for this is the greatest happiness for man.
「那位来自彼岸世界的信使还报告说，当时先知这样说道：『即便最后一个上前的人，只要选择明智、生活奋勉，也有一段可接受而非恶劣的人生为他保留。最先选择者不要轻率，最后选择者也不要灰心。』」
“And at that time also the messenger from that other world reported that the prophet spoke thus: ‘Even for him who comes forward last, if he make his choice wisely and live strenuously, there is reserved an acceptable life, no evil one. Let not the foremost in the choice be heedless nor the last be discouraged.’
先知说完后，厄尔说，抽中第一签的人立刻冲上前，攫取最大的僭主之位；他愚蠢而贪婪，没有充分考察就选择了它〔619c〕，没有看出其中包含吃掉自己孩子的命定，以及其他恐怖之事。等他从容细看，就捶胸顿足，为自己的选择哀号，把先知预先的警告抛在一旁。
When the prophet had thus spoken he said that the drawer of the first lot at once sprang to seize the greatest tyranny, and that in his folly and greed he chose it [619c] without sufficient examination, and failed to observe that it involved the fate of eating his own children, and other horrors, and that when he inspected it at leisure he beat his breast and bewailed his choice, not abiding by the forewarning of the prophet.
因为他没有为自己的灾祸责怪自己，反而责怪命运、诸神，以及除自己以外的一切。
For he did not blame himself for his woes, but fortune and the gods and anything except himself.
他原是从天上下来者之一，前一世生活在秩序良好的政体中〔619d〕，凭习惯而不是凭哲学分享德性。也许可以说，在这些中招的人里，大多数都来自天上，因为他们未曾受苦、缺少磨炼。反之，从地下上来的灵魂大多亲身受过苦，也见过他人的苦难，所以不会仓促选择。原书 p.238
He was one of those who had come down from heaven, a man who had lived in a well-ordered polity in his former existence, [619d] participating in virtue by habit and not by philosophy; and one may perhaps say that a majority of those who were thus caught were of the company that had come from heaven, inasmuch as they were unexercised in suffering. But the most of those who came up from the earth, since they had themselves suffered and seen the sufferings of others, did not make their choice precipitately.
正因如此，也因为抽签的机运，对大多数灵魂来说，善与恶发生了交替。
For which reason also there was an interchange of good and evil for most of the souls, as well as because of the chances of the lot.

出于惯性的选择Choice from Inertia

这是整个故事最有意思的转折之一：刚从地狱回来的人更可能作出好选择，从天堂回来的人反而更可能作出愚蠢选择！善者不会越来越善，恶者也不会越来越恶。教训的一部分似乎正是这一点：好运坏运与善恶并不重合；我们能够接受今生的坏运，并把它转向灵魂的善好。
This is one of the most interesting twists of the whole story - those who had just come from hell were more likely to make good choices, and those from heaven more likely to make foolish choices! The good don't keep getting gooder, and the bad don't keep getting badder. Part of the lesson seems to be precisely that point, that good and bad fortune do not coincide with good and evil, and that we can take bad fortune in this life and turn it towards goodness of soul.
然而，如果一个人每次返回这个世界的生命时〔619e〕都健全地热爱智慧，而他选择的签又不落在最后几号之中，那么依照来自彼岸的报告，我们可以大胆断言：他不只在这里会幸福，从此处前往彼岸、再返回这个世界的旅程，也不会穿行地下崎岖之路，而会平顺地经过诸天。
Yet if at each return to the life of this world [619e] a man loved wisdom sanely, and the lot of his choice did not fall out among the last, we may venture to affirm, from what was reported thence, that not only will he be happy here but that the path of his journey thither and the return to this world will not be underground and rough but smooth and through the heavens.
他说，观看各个灵魂怎样选择自己的生命，是一幅值得一看的景象〔620a〕。他说那是一幕奇异、可怜而可笑的场面，因为大多数灵魂的选择都由前世习惯决定。
For he said that it was a sight worth seeing to observe how the several souls selected their lives. [620a] He said it was a strange, pitiful, and ridiculous spectacle, as the choice was determined for the most part by the habits of their former lives.
我们从经验中学习，竟是如此缓慢！即便说到灵魂成长，我们大多也只是简单习惯的生物。这里再次强调此时此地有意识地生活：我们最终建立的是品格与灵魂品质，任何人生境遇都可以用来促进这种成长。
How slow we are to learn from experience! Even in terms of soul growth we are mostly creatures of simple habit. Again the emphasis on living consciously here and now, that what we are ultimately building is character and quality of soul, and that any life circumstance can be used to contribute to that growth.
奥德修斯Odysseus的灵魂恰好抽中了所有签中的最后一号。轮到选择时，他想起前世的劳苦，已经抛开野心，于是长久四处寻找一个安分守己的普通公民之生命；他费尽周折，终于发现它被其他灵魂忽略，丢在某个角落〔620d〕。一看见它，他便说，即使自己抽到第一签也会作同样选择，于是欣然选下。原书 p.239
And it fell out that the soul of Odysseus drew the last lot of all and came to make its choice, and, from memory of its former toils having flung away ambition, went about for a long time in quest of the life of an ordinary citizen who minded his own business, and with difficulty found it lying in some corner disregarded by the others, [620d] and upon seeing it said that it would have done the same had it drawn the first lot, and chose it gladly.
其他动物也同样如此：有的进入人的生命，有的彼此互换。不义者化为野兽，正义者化为驯兽，各种混合与组合无不具备。
And in like manner, of the other beasts some entered into men and into one another, the unjust into wild creatures, the just transformed to tame, and there was every kind of mixture and combination.
最后，所有灵魂按抽签顺序选完自己的生命，便列队走到拉刻西斯面前。她给每个灵魂派出由它选择的代蒙genius〔620e〕，作为一生的守护者与所选人生的实现者。这位神灵先把灵魂带到克洛托那里，在她手下、在她转动的纺锤中，确认其抽签与选择所定下的命定；接触克洛托后，代蒙又把灵魂带到阿特洛波斯转动的纺锤前，使命定之织物不可逆转；随后，灵魂头也不回地从必然女神的宝座下经过。
But when, to conclude, all the souls had chosen their lives in the order of their lots, they were marshaled and went before Lachesis. And she sent with each, [620e] as the guardian of his life and the fulfiller of his choice, the genius that he had chosen, and this divinity led the soul first to Clotho, under her hand and her turning of the spindle to ratify the destiny of his lot and choice; and after contact with her the genius again led the soul to the spinning of Atropos to make the web of its destiny irreversible, and then without a backward look it passed beneath the throne of Necessity.
术语genius 对应守护代蒙
乔伊特译本这里用 genius，所指是希腊语 daimōn：灵魂自己选择、随其投生并守护其一生的神灵。依本书术语合同译「代蒙」，不是基督宗教意义的恶魔。
〔621a〕经过宝座后，等其他灵魂也全部通过，他们一同前往忘却平原，穿过可怕而令人窒息的酷热，因为那里没有树木，也没有任何植物。黄昏时，他们在遗忘河畔扎营；没有任何容器能够盛装那条河的水。
[621a] And after it had passed through that, when the others also had passed, they all journeyed to the Plain of Oblivion, through a terrible and stifling heat, for it was bare of trees and all plants, and there they camped at eventide by the River of Forgetfulness, whose waters no vessel can contain.
所有灵魂都必须喝下定量的水；未受良好判断力拯救者，喝得超过规定分量。每个灵魂一喝下水，便忘记一切。
They were all required to drink a measure of the water, and those who were not saved by their good sense drank more than the measure, and each one as he drank forgot all things.
每个灵魂都必须饮下遗忘之水——但明智者喝得更少。这暗示，不断驱使我们来到这里的是对经验的渴望；也暗示，智慧会记得正在度过的生命是自己的选择，是自身欲望与价值的镜子——基本上，我们得到的是自己选择、自己想要的人生。原书 p.240
Each are required to drink the water of forgetfulness - but the wise ones drink less - implying that it is thirst for experience that keeps driving us here - and also implying that wisdom keeps in mind that the life we are living is our own choice - is a mirror of our desires and values - basically that we get the life that we choose, that we want.
我们通过实践自己的欲望与价值、从中学习而成长。随着我们横越多世而成长成熟，价值也会成长成熟；纯粹的尘世欲望或好运坏运被留在身后，真正的灵魂或灵性成长，才作为真实的善、唯一真实的价值留存。横跨多世的成长过程，与单独一段善度人生中的成长过程相互平行。
We learn by living out our desires and values, learning from them. As we grow and mature over the lives our values grow and mature, and mere earthly desire or good or bad fortune become left behind, and true soul or spiritual growth is left as the real good, the only real value. The growth process over multiple lives parallels the growth process in a single well-lived human lifetime.
〔621b〕他们睡着之后，到了午夜，雷声轰鸣，大地震动；灵魂突然被从那里托起，向不同方向飞升，投向各自的出生，如同流星。
[621b] And after they had fallen asleep and it was the middle of the night, there was a sound of thunder and a quaking of the earth, and they were suddenly wafted thence, one this way, one that, upward to their birth like shooting star.
厄尔说，自己没有获准饮下河水；但他不知道以何方式、沿怎样的路径返回了身体，只在突然恢复视力时，看见自己清晨正躺在葬礼柴堆上。
Er himself, he said, was not allowed to drink of the water, yet how and in what way he returned to the body he said he did not know, but suddenly recovering his sight he saw himself at dawn lying on the funeral pyre.
——因此，格劳孔，正如俗话所说，这个故事被保存下来，没有失传。
- And so, Glaucon, the tale was saved, as the saying is, and was not lost.
〔621c〕如果我们相信它，它也会拯救我们；我们将安全渡过勒忒河，不让灵魂沾染尘世污点。
[621c] And it will save us if we believe it, and we shall safely cross the River of Lethe, and keep our soul unspotted from the world.
但如果你愿意与我一同相信，灵魂不朽，能够承受善恶的一切极端，我们就会永远坚持向上之路，始终以智慧追求正义，使我们在此世旅居期间，以及领取奖赏之时〔621d〕，都能为自己和诸神所爱，正如竞赛胜者四处收集
But if you will believe with me that the soul is immortal and capable of enduring all extremes of good and evil, and so we shall hold ever to the upward way and pursue justice with wisdom always and ever, that we may be dear to ourselves and to the gods both during our sojourn here and when we receive our reward, [621d] as the victors in the games go about to gather
自己的奖品。如此，无论在此处，还是在我向你讲述的那场千年旅程中，我们都将一路安好。原书 p.241
in theirs. And thus both here and in that journey of a thousand years, whereof I have told you, we shall fare well.
厄尔神话至此结束，《理想国》也至此结束。
Here ends the Myth of Er, and the Republic.
我们在善恶方面种什么就收获什么，所以横越多世的成长，就是在正直与智慧中成长——两者是同义词。
We reap what we sow in terms of good and evil, so growth through the lives is growth in righteousness and wisdom - the two are synonyms.

结论Conclusions

下面是《理想国》以及整个柏拉图主义传统得出的一般结论。
Here are the general conclusions that are drawn in the Republic, and in the Platonic tradition in general.
作为人，我们拥有不朽灵魂——或者更准确地说，我们是目前正度过可朽生命的不朽意识存在。我们不是「拥有」灵魂；我们「就是」灵魂。
As human beings we have immortal souls - or better, we are immortal conscious beings currently living mortal lives. We don't HAVE souls, we ARE souls.
柏拉图主义者会把我们不朽的部分视为理性部分。我认为，今天所理解的理性含义过于局限；我把它看作我们作出有意识的伦理决定的部分，也是能够从自身可朽处境退后一步，判断应该如何行动的部分。
The part of us which is immortal is what Platonists would view as the rational part. I think that reason as we currently think of it has too limited a connotation. I think of it as the part of us that makes conscious and ethical decisions. It is also the part of us that can stand back from our mortal condition and judge how to act.
既然灵魂不朽，这就意味着我们通过一系列生命成长与演化。在我们的生命中，能超越今生而存续的，是伦理与道德的部分，关乎以正义或不义方式行动；正是这个部分作出价值判断。
Given that the soul is immortal, this means that we grow and evolve through a series of lives. The part of our lives that survives this one life is our ethical and moral part, and concerns our acting with justice or injustice. It is the part that makes value judgments.
人生的基本形态由我们依自身欲望选择——我们选择自己人生的基本形态。这意味着，我们之所以处于今生这个特定生命条件，是因为曾为特定目的选择一组经验。这个目的既属于作为个体的我们，也属于作为更大人类集体一部分的我们；我们在个体与集体层面成长、改变并演化。
The basic shape of our lives is chosen by us according to what we desire - we choose the basic shape of our lives. This means that we are here in this particular life condition because there is a set of experiences we have chosen for a specific purpose. This purpose is both for us as individuals, and for us as part of the larger human collective - we are growing, changing, and evolving, individually and collectively.
我们选择的生命与自己的价值和欲望对齐；选择可以或多或少经过深思、推理并保持自觉。与人生中的任何单独事件一样，我们的选择与行动可能谨慎周详，也可能欠缺思考、冲动而为。厄尔神话特意描述了一个未经思考便扑向某段人生，继而为选择哀号的人；他责怪诸神，责怪命定——责怪除自己以外的一切。
The life we choose aligns with our values and desires, and our choices can be more or less thoughtful, reasoned and conscious. As in any single event in our lives, our choices and actions may be careful and thought out, or thoughtless and impulsive. The Myth of Er story specifically describes someone jumping into a life without thought and then bewailing their choice, blaming the Gods, blaming fate - blaming anything except themselves.
由此可知，人生整体目的的核心部分正是这个成长过程：学习我们的欲望与价值所包含的意义及其后果。如同儿童长大成人，我们的欲望与需要会成长改变。随着经验增多，并且（希望如此，却未必必然）变得更具自觉，我们的价值感与行为会改变成熟——不过我要说，缺乏自觉可以视为较年轻或不成熟，发展成熟的自觉则可视为较进阶或成熟。原书 p.242
It follows that a central part of the overall purpose of human life is precisely this growth process, learning the implications and consequences of our desires and values. Like children growing into adults, our desires and needs grow and change. Our sense of values and our conduct changes and matures as we have more experience and (hopefully but not necessarily) become more self-aware - though I would say that lack of self-awareness can be viewed as younger or more immature, and developed self-awareness can be viewed as more advanced or mature.
这种价值觉知的成长，是横跨多世整个过程的一项元价值：具有自觉，以成熟、审慎、理性而有意识的方式选择自身价值。
This growth in awareness of values is a meta-value to the entire process that spans lives - self-awareness, choosing our values maturely, deliberately, rationally and consciously.
哲学之路正是在这里进入，作为一条通向自觉的道路；占星学也在这里进入。
This is where the path of philosophy comes in, as being a path of self-awareness - and this is also where astrology comes in.

那么，占星学呢？What of Astrology?

这一切怎样与占星学联系起来？
How does this tie in with astrology?
占星学只有放在哲学生活的语境中才有意义。对我来说，这意味着占星学必须包含对价值判断的觉知成长。与我合作的大多数人，对「自己将发生什么」的关心较少，对「为什么」的问题更关心，也就是目的与意义的问题。
Astrology only makes sense in the context of the philosophic life. To me this means that astrology needs to include growth in awareness of value judgments. Most people I work with are less concerned with what will happen to them, and more concerned with the Why questions, questions of purpose and meaning.
既然人生有目的，那么问题就变成：怎样以最好的方式与我们抽中的人生之签合作、回应它，从而促进德性与自觉的成长。这会使我们受益，使他人受益，也使社会受益。最终，以一种通常超越我们理解能力的方式，我们的人生会纳入一个更大的宇宙秩序。
Given that there is a purpose to human life, the question then becomes how we can best work with, and respond to, the lot in life that we are cast, in such a way as to further our growth in virtue and self-awareness. This benefits us, it benefits others and it benefits our society. Ultimately, in a way beyond we can usually conceive, our life fits within a larger cosmic order.
多层秩序彼此映照这一概念，是《理想国》的根本原则之一。人的个体德性映照在政治领域有德性的结构中，后者又映照诸天的宇宙秩序。我们研究占星学，是为了使自己与宇宙秩序——其中包括道德秩序——对齐。原书 p.243
This concept of the multiple levels of order all mirroring each other is one of the foundation principles of the Republic. Individual human virtue is mirrored in a virtuous structure in the political arena, and this in turn is a mirror of the cosmic order of the heavens. We study astrology to align ourselves with cosmic order, which includes a moral order.
占星学怎样提供帮助？它帮助我们醒来，帮助我们经过思考看清自己的欲望与价值，有意识地投入这场价值成长过程，从而作出更明智的选择与行动。
How does astrology help? By helping us to wake up, to engage in this process of growth in values consciously by thinking through our desires and values so that we may choose and act more wisely.
这就是占星学的目的——帮助我们以明智而自觉的方式，选择应当如何生活，并为这些选择负责。
This is the purpose of astrology - to help us to make wise and self-aware choices of how to live our lives, and to take responsibility for those choices.
回想故事：命运三女神向我们提供可供选择的生命；同样是她们，在柏拉图的必然之纺锤内，编织并掌管那份命定在一生中的结构与展开——这就是诸天环绕下的地上生命，而整个宇宙结构都映照在占星学中。
Recall from the story that it is the Fates who offer us the lives from which to choose, and it is the Fates who weave and control the structure and unfolding of that fate through a life within Plato's Spindle of Necessity - life on earth surrounded by the heavens, the whole cosmic structure mirrored in astrology.
占星学中行星的运动与秩序，显示那套秩序——换一种说法，就是我们的命定——如何展开。我们永远需要记住，命定并不决定我们选择正义还是不义地行动；这永远在我们的选择之内，而有时它就是我们唯一能够选择的事。
The movement and order of the planets in astrology shows the unfolding of that order - or, in other terms, our fate. We always need to remember that fate does NOT determine whether we choose to act justly or unjustly - that is always within our choice - and sometimes that is all that is within our choice.
这种随时间与经验增长的觉知，引向整篇《理想国》对话录的中心前提。简单说，正直无私地行动是明智的，自私欺骗地行动是不明智的。放进横跨多世的更大视野，德性自有回报，恶习自会惩罚——最终只有这一点重要。人生中唯一真正重要的事，都关乎不朽灵魂的状态与成长。
This growth in awareness over time and experience leads to the conclusion that is the central premise of the entire Republic dialog. Simply put, it is wise to act righteously and unselfishly, and it is unwise to act selfishly and dishonestly. In the larger multi-lifetime view of things, virtue pays and vice punishes - and ultimately that is all that matters. The only truly important things in life are those concerned with the state and growth of our immortal souls.
这预设宇宙内置道德秩序——存在并非道德中立——并且宇宙的底层结构就是正义。正义不是我们身为人类覆盖在道德中立的物质宇宙之上的某种附加层。正义结构直接嵌在一切创造赖以建立的最基本本原中。用柏拉图主义术语说，正义是首要的柏拉图理型之一；换一种说法，正义是最根本、最首要的神明之一。原书 p.244
This assumes there is a built in moral order to the universe - that existence is not morally neutral - and, that the underlying structure of the universe is one of justice. Justice is not some kind of overlay that we place as humans on top of a morally neutral material universe. The structure of justice is wired right into the very basic principles on which all of creation is built. In Platonic terms, justice is one of the primary platonic forms - or in other terms, justice is one of the most basic and primary of the gods.
太一与至善——道德善好、正义、公平、慈悲——都是宇宙的性质，其根基地位如此深刻，甚至先于存在本身，并位于存在之下。我们是那太一的一部分；在这里的部分目的，就是醒来认识这一事实，并永远在这个语境中行动。
The One and the Good - moral goodness, justice, fairness, mercy, are properties of our universe that are so foundational that they precede and underlay even existence itself. We are part of that Oneness, and part of our purpose here is to wake up to that fact, and to always act within that context.
提示命定与自由的边界
行星秩序显示人生之签如何在时间中展开，却不决定灵魂选择正义还是不义。作者由此把传统占星学的命定判断与柏拉图伦理连接起来：外在境遇未必可控，回应的品质仍属自由；占星学的目的不是取消命定，而是帮助人更清醒地生活于其中。
Charles Obert, Saturn Through the Ages: Between Time and Eternity, Minneapolis: Almuten Press, 2019.
